ivvaruṅkalaip payiṟciyil muṟkālattē naṉku paḻakiya nantamiḻantaṇarkaḷ, āriyar tam veṟiyāṭṭu vēḷvikaḷiṟ kalantu. avarkaḷ veṭṭum āṭu māṭu kutirai mutalāṉa vilaṅkukaḷ avarkaḷāl veṭṭuppaṭutaṟkuc ciṟitu muṉṉarē yuṇarviḻantu veṭṭuppaṭumāṟu ceytu, avai an nērattil aṭaiya irunta poṟuttaṟkariya nōyt tuṉpattaip pōkki vanta pēraruṭṭiṟam peritu! peritu! ivvāṟāka vēḷviyiṟ kollappaṭṭa vilaṅkiṉaṅkaḷ uṇarviḻantu nōviṉṟi yuyir viṭumāṟu ceytamaikku irukkuvēta mutaṉ maṇṭilattiṉ 142 ām patikattiṟ pōnta,
nī tēvarkaḷpāṟ celkaiyil niṉ uyirvaḷi niṉṉai varuttātatāka! kōṭariyāṉatu niṉ uṭampai ūṟu paṭuttāta tāka! tiṟamiṉṟi viraintu veṭṭit taṉṟoḻiliṟ peritu piḻaipaṭuṅ kolaiñaṉ niṉuṟuppukaḷaip piḻaipaṭap piḷavāṉāka! meyyākavē nī iṅkēiṟantu paṭukiṉṟilai! nī ētoru tuṉpamum aṭaikiṉṟilai! nī eḷiya iṉiya vaḻiyāl tēvarkaḷ pāṟ celkiṉṟaṉai! eṉṉum uṟutiyuraikaḷē cāṉṟākum. iññāṉṟum makkaḷaiyum piṟavuyirkaḷaiyum uṇarviḻakkac ceyyum aṟituyil vallār aṭuttaṭuttuk kūṟi aṅṅaṉam uṇar viḻappiṉai (Anaesthesia) varuvikkum uṟutik kaṭṭurai kaḷō ṭoppa mēṟkāṭṭiya patikak kaṭṭuraikaḷ amain tiruttaluṅ karuttiṟ patikkaṟpāṟṟu ivvāṟāka muṉṉirunta tamiḻ muṉivarkaḷ, āriyar ceytu pōnta uyirkkolai vēḷvikaḷil tāmum uṭaṉpāṭuṭaiyar pōṟ kalantu niṉṟu avar avvēḷvik kaḷaṅkaḷil veṭṭi vīḻkkum vilaṅ kiṉaṅkaḷai veṭṭuṇṇuṅ koṭun tuṉpam aṟiyāmal uṇarviḻakkac ceytu, cila kālamellām avaruṭaiya vēḷvikaḷaiyum naṭātti vantaṉar. vantu meṉ!
paṭaikoṇṭār neñcampōl naṉṟūkkā toṉṟaṉ
uṭalcuvai yuṇṭār maṉam	(kuṟaḷ 253)
eṉṉun teyvat tirukkuṟaḷ kūṟiyāṅku, ūṉcuvaiyiṟ paḻakiya āriyar tāñ ceytupōnta uyirkkolai vēḷviyiṉaic ciṟitum viṭārāyiṉar!
atukaṇṭa tamiḻantaṇarun tamiḻaracarum avvuyirk kolai vēḷvikaḷ mēṉmēṟ ṟīviṉaiyaiyē perukkum eṉavum; ellāmvalla āṇṭavaṉ malattāl aṟivu maṟaippuṇṭa ellāvuyirkaḷukkum ammalamaṟaippiṉai nīkki avai tamakku aṟivum iṉpamun tarutaṟ poruṭ ṭākavē uṭampukaḷaip pēraruḷiṉāṟ paṭaittuk koṭuttirukka, avaṉatu aruṭṭaṉmaikku māṟāka avaṉ paṭaitta avvuṭampukaḷaic citaittu avvayirkaḷai aṟiyāmaiyilun tuṉpattilum amiḻttutal avvāṇṭa vaṉukkut taṇittaṟkariya peruñ cīṟṟattiṉai mūṭṭuvatā meṉavum; iṟaivaṉatu aruḷiyalpiṉaiyum ataṉaip peṟutaṟkuriya ciṟṟuyirkaḷākiya nāmippōtirukkum maruḷiyalpiṉaiyum naṉkuṇarntu pārttu, aruḷukku māṟāṉa maruḷaiyellām aṟavē yoḻittu, ellā vuyirkaḷiṭattum avaṉai yoppa aruḷuṭaṉ oḻuki avaṉṟaṉ aruṭperun taṉmaiyiṉai ōvātu niṉaintu niṉaintu kaṉintu kaṉintu urukutalāl maṭṭumē aṟiyāmai tēyntu aruḷaṟivu nampāṟ cuṭarntoḷirumeṉavum ataṉāl immai maṟumai yiraṇṭilum iyamputaṟkariya iṉpavāḻkkaiyē ellā vuyirkkum vāykkumallatu tuṉpameṉpatu ciṟitun talaikkāṭṭāteṉavum; ākavē uyirkkolai vēḷvikaḷaik kaṟpikkum āriya vētap pakutikaḷum avaṟṟiṟkūṟiya vāṟu ceyyuñ ciṟu teyva veṟiyāṭṭukaḷuṅ kaḻiperun tīmai payappavākaliṉ avaṟṟai yaruvaruttu vilakkutalē ceyaṟpāla teṉavum vaṟpuṟuttic `cākkiyam, `yōkam mutalāṉa aṟivu nūlkaḷum, `īca, `kēna, `kaṭam, `piracinam, `muṇṭakam `māṇṭūkyam `taittirīyam `cāntōkyam `pirukatāraṇ yakam `cuvētā cuvataram mutalāṉa  upaniṭataṅkaḷum iyaṟṟiṉar. ivaṟṟuṭaṉ nillāmal, ellāmvalla muḻumutaṟ poruḷ civam oṉṟēyātalaiyum āriyarāl vaṇaṅkappaṭṭa intiraṉ, varuṇaṉ, cōmaṉ, piramaṉ, viṇṭu mutaliyōrellām piṟantiṟakkuñ ciṟṟuyirkaḷē yātalaiyum pakuttaṟivu kūṭāp potumakkaṭku naṉku teruṭṭutaṟ poruṭṭu, avar aṟitaṟku eḷiyavāṉa purāṇak kataikaḷ cilavum puṉain turaip poruḷāyp paṭaitteḻutalāyiṉar.
iṉṉum, ivaṟṟuṭaṉ amaiti peṟāmal, uyirk kolaikkum veṟiyāṭṭukkum iṭaṉāka āriyarāṟ ṟiripu paṭuttappaṭṭa tīvēṭṭalaiyē muṟṟum oḻippāṉ vēṇṭit, tī vaḷarkkuṅ kuḻikku aṟikuṟiyāka oru vaṭṭakkal vaṭivum, akkuḻiyil vaḷarkkappaṭun tīyiṉukku aṟikuṟiyākak kuvintu nīṇṭatoru kalvaṭivum vakuttu avai yiraṇ ṭaṉaiyum oruṅku porutti niṟuttiya civaliṅkattiṉetirē muṉṉē vēḷviyil veṭṭak koṇarnta māṭṭiṉukku aṟikuṟiyāka oru nantiyuruvi ṉaiyum amaittu, muṉṉē ammāṭṭiṉai veṭṭiya pali mēṭaikku aṟikuṟiyāka annantiyuruviṉ pakkattē oru palipīṭattiṉaiyum eḻuppic civapirāṉ ṟiruk kōyilkaḷai āṅkāṅku eṭuppikka lāyiṉar.
ivvāṟu civaliṅka aruṭkuṟiyum ataṉetirē nantiyuruvum ataṉ piṉṉē palipīṭamum amaittut tiruk kōyilkaḷ uṇṭākkiya piṉ, muṉṉē āriyar ceytu pōnta veṟiyāṭṭu vēḷviyum avaṟṟiṉkaṇ naṭanta uyirkkolaiyum niṉaivil varāmaipporuṭṭu, am mūṉṟu kuṟikaḷum mūṉṟu meyp poruḷaṭaiyāḷaṅ kaḷāka vakukkappaṭṭaṉa veṉṟu avai tamakku viḻumiya meyp poruḷuṅ kūṟalāyiṉar, iṟaivaṉ ṟiruvaṭai yāḷamē civaliṅkamāmeṉavum, iṟaivaṉallāta ciṟṟuyiriṉ aṭai yāḷamē nantivaṭivāmeṉavum, avvuyiriṉāṟ kaḻikkap paṭṭa aṟiyāmaiyākiya malamē palipīṭamā meṉavum naṉku viḷakkip pati, pacu, pāca uṇmaiyiṉai aṟivikkum poruṭṭē civaliṅkamum nantiyum palipīṭamum amaitta tirukkōyilkaḷ eṭukkappaṭṭaṉa veṉṟu paṇṭirunta tamiḻc cāṉṟōrkaḷ tirukkōyilamaippiṉai mika vuyarnta meyyaṟivu muṟaiyilē vaittut tiruppiviṭa lāyiṉar, iṅṅaṉam uyarnta muṟaiyil vaittut tiruk kōyilamaippu viḷakkappaṭṭa uṇmaiyiṉai, iṟṟaikku āyirattu nāṉūṟu āṇṭukaṭku muṉṉirunta aruntava māmuṉivarākiya āciriyar tirumūlar,
āya patitāṉ aruṭciva liṅkamām
āya pacuvum aṭalē ṟeṉaniṟkum
āya palipīṭam ākunaṟ pācam
āya araṉnilai āyntukoḷ vārkkē (ceyyuḷ 2411)
eṉṟu tamatu tirumantira nūlil aruḷic ceytavāṟṟāl naṉkaṟintukoḷka. tirukkōyilil iṟaivaṉait toḻutaṟkuc cellum oruvar palipīṭattiṉarukē ceṉṟatun tamakkuḷḷa yāṉ eṉatu eṉṉum iruvakaic ceruk kiṉaiyum veṭṭip paliyākac celuttutaṟku aṭaiyāḷa mākavē avar ataṉ pakkattē nīḷakkiṭantu vaṇaṅku talākum, aṅṅaṉam vaṇaṅkiya avaratu uyir ataṉāl avviruvakaic cerukkun tīrntu tūyatāki iṟaivaṉai nōkkic cellum aṭaiyāḷamākavē pacuviṉ vaṭiviṉa tāṉa nanti civaliṅkattiṉetirē niṟuttappaṭṭa tākum, annantiyum annantiyiṉetirē niṟkuñ civaliṅkamum kalvaṭiviṟ kāṇappaṭiṉum, accivaliṅka vaṭivu tūya oḷi yuruvil muṉaittu niṟkuñ civamāmeṉavum, avvoḷi yuruvil oṉṟi iraṇṭaṟak kalakkun tūyavuyir atiṟ karaintu kalakkumāṟu ituvāmeṉavuṅ kāṭṭutaṟ poruṭṭē iṟaivaṉait toḻap pōvār muṉṉilai yiṟ karuppura voḷikāṭṭum muṟaiyiṉait tamiḻc cāṉṟōr vakukka lāyiṉar. karuppuran tūya veṇṇiṟat tatā yiruttaliṉ, iruvakaip paṟṟum viṭṭu iṟaivaṉō ṭoṉṟi iraṇṭaṟak kalakkap pōntūyavuyirkku aḵtu aṭaiyāḷamāyiṟṟu; akkaruppurattiṟ paṟṟiya nerup poḷi akkaruppurattaik kavarntu karaittut taṉatākkum nilai, oḷiyuruviṉaṉāṉa iṟaivaṉai attūyavuyiraip paṟṟit taṉatākkum nikaḻccikku aṭaiyāḷamāyiṟṟu, ivvarum pēruṇmai muṟaiyiṉaiyē tavamāc celvarāṉa tāyumāṉa aṭikaḷ,
tāṉāṉa taṉmayamē yallāl oṉṟait
	talaiyeṭukka voṭṭātu talaippaṭ ṭāṅkē
pōṉāluṅ karppūra tīpam pōlap
	pōyoḷippa tallātu pulamvē ṟiṉṟām
ñāṉākā rattiṉoṭu ñēya maṟṟa
	ñāturuvum naḻuvāmal naḻuvi niṟkum
āṉālum itaṉperumai evarkkār colvār
	atuvāṉāl atuvāvar atuvē collum
eṉṟum,
oruvaṉavaṉ yāṉaikeṭak kuṭattuṭ ceṅkai
	ōṭṭutalpōl nāṉpētaiyuppō ṭappai
maruvaviṭṭuṅ karppūra mataṉiṟ ṟīpam
	vayaṅkaviṭṭum aikkiyamuṉṉi varuntiniṟpēṉ
aruḷuṭaiya parameṉṟō aṉṟu tāṉē
	yāṉuḷaṉeṉ ṟumeṉakkē yāṇa vāti
perukuviṉaik kaṭṭeṉṟum eṉṉāṟ kaṭṭip
	pēciyataṉ ṟēyaruṇūl pēciṟ ṟaṉṟē
eṉṟum ākāra puvaṉañ citampara rakaciyattil, (22, 29) aruḷic ceytiruttal kaṇṭukoḷka.
iṅṅaṉamellām pōnta tirukkōyil meyp poruḷ viḷakkamuṟai āciriyar tirumūlar kālattiṟkuc ciṟitu muṉṉē tuvaṅkip pōnta taṉṟi ataṟku muṟpaṭṭa kālattē vaḻaṅkiya taṉṟeṉpatu, paṇṭaic centamiḻilak kiyaṅkaḷil atu kāṇappaṭāmaiyāṟ ṟeṟṟeṉat tuṇiyap paṭukiṉṟatu,.
maṟṟup, paṇṭai nāḷil āriyar ceytu pōnta uyirkkolai vēḷviyai aṭiyoṭu niṟuttit, tiruk kōyil eṭuppitta tollāciriyar muttīvēṭṭa vēḷvikkuḻikku aṟikuṟiyākavē vaṭṭakkal vaṭivum, atil vaḷartta aṉaṟpiḻampukku aṟikuṟiyākavē atu pōl nīṇṭu kuvintatoru kalvaṭivum vakuttu, avai yiraṇṭaiyum poruttiya civaliṅkattiṉetirē, muṟ kālattu āriya vēḷvikkaḷattil veṭṭuṇṭa erutukku aṟikuṟiyākavē erutuvaṭivāṉa nantiyai niṟutti, av verutu veṭṭuṇṭa palimēṭaikku aṟikuṟiyākavē ataṉaiyotta pali pīṭattai eḻuppic civapirāṉṟiruk kōyil amaittaṉa reṉka.
paṇṭai nāḷil malaikaḷiṉ mītirunta tamiḻ muṉ ṉōrkaḷ muttīvēṭṭalaik kaiviṭṭapiṉ avaṟṟiṉ mēl eṭuppitta tirukkōyilkaḷē mikappaḻaiyaṉa, avai: tirupparaṅkuṉṟam, tirukkoṭuṅkuṉṟam, tirukkaḻuk kuṉṟam, tirukkoṭimāṭac ceṅkuṉṟam tiruvaṇṇā malai, tiruvīṅkōymalai, tirunoṭittāṉmalai mutaliyaṉa.
piṉṉar malaikaḷiṉiṉṟuṅ kīḻiṟaṅkik kāṭukaḷil vaiki vāḻnta ñāṉṟu avarkaḷ amaippitta tiruk kōyilkaḷ, malaikkōyilkaḷukku aṭuttapaṭiyiṟ paḻaimaiyuṭaiyaṉa vākum, avai: tiruvālaṅkāṭu, tirumaṟaikkāṭu, tiruveṇ kāṭu, tiruccāykkāṭu, tiruppaṉaṅkāṭu, tirukkaccineṟik kāraikkāṭu mutaliyaṉa vākum.
ataṉpiṟ kāṭukaḷaiviṭṭu akaṉṟu uyirvāḻtaṟ kiṉiya iḷamarakkākkaḷilum poḻilkaḷilum pukuntu uṟainta ñāṉṟu avarkaḷ kaṭṭuvitta tirukkōyilkaḷ, kāṉakak kōyilkaḷukku aṭuttapaṭiyiṟ paḻaimai vāyntaṉa vākum, avai: tirukkaṭampu, tiruttillai, tiruvāṉaikkā, tirukkurakkukkā, tirukkōṭikā, tiruk kōlakkā, tirunellikkā, tiruvālampoḻil mutalāyiṉa.
ataṉpiṉ uḻavut toḻiṟkēṟṟa vaḷaviya marutanilam pukuntu avarkaḷ nilaipeṟṟa nākarika vāḻkkai yuṭaiyarāya piṟṟaiñāṉṟu eṭuppitta tirukkōyilkaḷ mēṟ kāṭṭiya kōyilkaḷukkup piṟpaṭṭa paḻaimai yuṭaiyaṉavākum, avai; tiruvārūr, tiruviṭaimarutūr, tiruvoṟṟiyūr, tirukkaṭavūr, tirukkaruvūr, tirunāva lūr, tiruppukalūr, tiruppuṉkūr, tirumayilāppūr, tiruvāṉmiyūr mutalāṉa iṉṉum palavākum
iṅṅaṉamāka, malai, kāṭu, kā, poḻil mutalāṉa iṭaṅkaḷai uṟaiviṭamāyk koṇṭu, ellām valla  iṟaivaṉai neruppuruvil vaittu vaṇaṅkiyapōtum, neruppuruvaik kaiviṭṭu ataṉōṭotta kalvaṭivu maṇvaṭivil vaittu iṟaivaṉai vaṇaṅkiyapōtum, avar avvaṇakkattaic ceytatu oru marattiṉ nīḻalilē yākum, tiruppuliyūr eṉṉuñ citamparattil tillai marattiṉ kīḻum, tiruvālavāyiṟ kaṭampamarattiṉ kīḻum, tiruvāṉaikkāvil nāvalmarattiṉ kīḻum, tiruppātirip puliyūriṟ pātiri marattiṉ kīḻum tirumayilāppūriṟ puṉṉaimarattiṉ kīḻum, tiruvoṟṟiyūril makiḻamarattiṉ kīḻum iṉṉum ivvāṟē ovvoru tirukkōyilum ovvoru marattiṉ kīḻum amaintiruttalai uṟṟu nōkkavallārkkup paṇṭirunta tamiḻ mēṉmakkaḷa ṉaivarum ellām valla civapirāṉai mara niḻalil vaittē vaḻipāṭāṟṟi vantamai teṟṟeṉa viḷaṅkā niṟkum. 
ā! malaimēlum, nilattiṉ kīḻuṅ, kāṭukaḷiluṅ kākkaḷilum marattiṉkīḻ muttī vaḷarttum, attīppiḻampiṟ kaṟikuṟiyāṉa civaliṅkam niṟuttiyum iṟaivaṉai nantamiḻ mēṉmakkaḷ vaḻipaṭṭu vanta ñāṉṟu avaruḷḷam ettuṇai āṟutaluṭaiyatāy, ettuṇai amaitiyuṭaiya tāy, ettuṇai oḷiviḷakkam aruḷ viḷakkam vāynta tāyt tikaḻntiruttal vēṇṭum! aruḷ oḷi vayattarāy niṉṟu avar vaḻipaṭṭuvanta oḷiyuruvum, ataṉaiyotta civaliṅka vaṭivumē piṟappiṟappillā mutalvaṉai vaḻi paṭutaṟ kiyainta uṇmai vaṭivaṅkaḷākum, iṟaivaṟ kuṇmaiyāṉa ivvaruṭ kuṟi vaṭivaiviṭṭu, iṟaivaṉuṇ maikku māṟāṉa vaṭivukaḷai avaravar tattam maṉampōṉa vāṟellām vakuttu vaittuk koṇṭu vaḻipaṭuvarāyiṉ, avar iruḷulakam pukuvareṉa īcāvāciyōpaniṭatam (9) pukalutalāṟ, civaliṅka vaṭiviṉaiyē ellārum vaḻipaṭṭu iṟaivaṉṟaṉ aruḷulakam pukuvārāka!
aṭikkuṟippukaḷ
1.	Louis Kuhne; see his “New Science of Healing”.
2.	Eliphas Levi’s The Mysteries of Magic. p. 84.
3.	Harmsworth History of the world, p. 5867.
4.	ivai tammai vaṭanūlār mūlātāram, vātiṣṭāṉam, maṇipūrakam, anākatam, vicutti, āññai eṉa vaḻaṅkuvar.
5.	(pāṭam) kuyyam.
6.	yātē rutra civātanu rakōrā pāpakācinī, tayā natanuvā cantamayā kiricantāpicāka cīhi cuvētācuvatarōpa niṣat, 3, 5.
7.	paṭaippiṉṟōṟṟum (Genesis), 22.
8.	Read “Mesmerism in India” by James Esdaild, M.D., and “Hypnotism” by Carl Sextus.



9. ampalavāṇar tirukkūttiṉ uṇmai
epporuṭkum evvuyirkkum uḷḷakattum puṟattum
	iyaluṇmai aṟiviṉpa vaṭivāki naṭikkum
meypporuḷām civam oṉṟē eṉṟaṟintēṉ uṉakkum
	viḷampukiṉṟēṉ maṭavāynī kiḷampukiṉṟāy mīṭṭum
ipporuḷap poruḷeṉṟē icaippateṉṉē potuvil
	iṟaivarceyyum niraticaya iṉpanaṭan taṉainī
paippaṟavē kāṇutiyēl attaruṇat tellām
	paṭṭanaṭup pakalpōla veṭṭaveḷi yāmē
- irāmaliṅka aṭikaḷ
irāmaliṅka aṭikaḷār aruḷic ceyta mēlait tiruppāṭṭiṉai mēṟkōḷāka eṭuttuk koṇṭu ampalavāṇar tirukkūttiṉuṇmaiyum ñāṉayōkamum eṉṉum ematu virivuraiyiṉai nikaḻttuvāṉ pukukiṉṟēm, aṟivillā evvakaippaṭṭa poruḷkaḷiṉakattum puṟattum, aṟivuṭaiya evvakaiyuyirkaḷiṉ akattum puṟattum niṟaintiruppatum,iyaṟkai yuṇmaiyumiyaṟkai yaṟivum iyaṟkai yiṉpamum uṭaiyatāki ovātu iyaṅkun tirukkūttai iyaṟṟuvatum āṉa uṇmaip poruḷāñ civam oṉṟē muḻumutaṟ kaṭavuḷākum eṉpatūum, itaṉuṇmaiyai yuṇarntu avvāṟṟāṟcivavayamāy niṉṟavarkkuttām pēriṉpattai yaṭainta avvaḷavil maṉavamaiti varātu tām peṟṟa peṟalarum pēṟṟai atu peṟāta ēṉai makkaḷum peṟumpaṭi avarkku ataṉai yeṭuttuk kūṟum aruḷirakka muṇṭāmeṉpatūum, aṅṅaṉañ civañāṉikaḷāyiṉār kūṟum meyyuraiyiṉai ēṟṟu nalampeṟum nalviṉai vāyātār tamakkuḷḷa āṇava aṟiyāmai muṉaippāl am meyyuraiyiṉai viṭuttu muḻumutaṟ kaṭavuḷallāta ciṟṟuyirkaḷaiyum uyiral poruḷkaḷaiyun teyvameṉattuṇintu itutāṉ kaṭavuḷ atutāṉ kaṭavuḷ eṉa icaittut tam piṟavippayaṉai iḻappareṉpatūum, maṟṟu aṅṅaṉam aṟiyāmai vayap paṭṭār pōlātu meyyuṇarntār kūṟum meyyurai yiṉai ēṟṟu ataṉvaḻi niṟkavalla nallārkku evvuyirk kum potuvāy aruḷ veḷiyilē āṭalpuriyum ampala vāṇaṉ iṉpak kūttiṉai niṉaivu oruṅki uṇarntu piṟavit tuṉpamaṟak kāṇum pēriṉpak kāṭci vāyttaliṉ avarkku uyiriṉiyalpu, ulakiṉiyalpu, malattiṉiyalpu mutalāyiṉavellām paṭṭappakaliṟ kaṇṇetirē kāṇap paṭum poruḷpōl naṉku pulaṉāmeṉpatūum mēlait tiruppāṭṭil naṉkeṭuttu nuvalappaṭutal kāṇka.
iṉi, ivaitammai muṟaiyē viḷaṅka virittu ārāyntu kāṇappukuvām. ulakattil nammaṟiviṟkup pulaṉākum poruḷkaḷellām aṟivil poruḷum aṟivuṭaiya uyirpporuḷum eṉṉumiruperuṅ kūṟṟil vantaṭaṅkuvaṉa vākum, ivaitammuḷ, aṟivil poruḷ kaḷeṉpaṉa tāmē iyaṅkamāṭṭāvāyp piṟitoṉṟu iyakkiṉāl iyaṅkun taṉmaiyavākum,iṉi, aṟivuṭaiya uyirkaḷeṉpaṉa tāmē aṟivaṉavāyum piṟar aṟivittaṉa vaṟṟai aṟivaṉavāyum piṟarkku aṟivikkavallaṉavāyum uyiraṟṟa poruḷkaḷai iyakkavallaṉavāyumirukkum eṉavē, uyirillāp poruḷiṉ ṟaṉmaiyum uyiruṭaiya poruḷiṉ ṟaṉmaiyum vevvēṟāy oṉṟiṉoṉṟu maṟutalaip paṭṭu niṟṟaliṉ,ivai ekkālattum evviṭattum oṉṟiṉoṉṟu vēṟāṉa iyaṟkaiyuṭaiya vākavē irukku mallāmal, aṟivil poruḷ aṟivuṭaip poruḷākavum, aṟivuṭaip poruḷ aṟivil poruḷākavum māṟutal ekkālattum evviṭattum nikaḻa māṭṭāteṉka
aṟṟēl, mēṉāṭṭavaril iyaṟkaip poruḷā rāycciyiṟ ciṟantārāṉa `pāṭiyaṉ (Bastian) eṉpār, aṟivillāta parupporuḷiṉiṉṟē aṟivuṭaiya uyirp poruḷ tōṉṟukiṉṟateṉap palamukattāṉārāyntu kūṟiyateṉṉai yeṉiṉ; avar ceyta ārāycci uṇmai yuṭaiyataṉṟu, ēṉeṉṟāl, avar ceyta avvārāycci yiṉait tirumpavum nuṇuki yārāynta ṭiṇṭal (Tyndall) eṉṉum pērāciriyar tāmārāyntu kaṇṭa muṭipāka, uyirillāp poruḷiliruntu uyiruṭaiya poruḷ tōṉṟāteṉṟum, uyiruḷḷa poruṭ cērkkai yiliruntē piṟitōruyir tōṉṟukiṉṟateṉṟum aṟi vuṟutti yuṟuti kūṟiyirukkiṉṟār1 ākavē, aṟivillāp parupporuḷi liruntu aṟivuṭaiya nuṇporuḷ tōṉṟu meṉṟal uṇmai yārāyccikku ovvāteṉṟu uṇartal vēṇṭum.
iṉi, ēkāṉmavātaṅkūṟum oru cārār caṭameṉṟuñ citteṉṟumiraṇṭillai; caṭamē cittu, cittē caṭam, piramattiṉiṉṟē neruppiṟ pukaipōla māyaiyākiya caṭapporuḷuṇṭām eṉṟu kūṟuvar, avar kūṟṟu mēlē kāṭṭiya uṇmaiyārāyccikku ovvāmaiyāṉum; neruppiluṇṭākum pukai neruppai yaṇainta viṟakiṉ kāriyamēyallātu neruppiṉ kāriyam aṉṟeṉpataṟkuk kaṉinta taṇaliṟ pukaiyuṇṭākak kāṇāmaiyē cāṉṟāmāka liṉ iv vuvamaiyiṉ taṉmaiyai yuṟṟuṇarātu, itaṉaik koṇṭu tūya aṟivup poruḷākiya piramattiṉiṉṟu aṟivillāc caṭapporuḷākiya māyai tōṉṟumeṉṟal oruvāṟṟāṉum poruntāmaiyāṉum ēkāṉmavātikaḷ kūṟum poruḷ ēṟkaṟpālataṉṟu. eṉavē, aṟivillāp poruḷkaḷum aṟivuṭaiya uyirp poruḷkaḷum ek kālattum vevvēṟām iyalpuṭaiyavākavē yirukku meṉṟu kaṭaippiṭittuṇartal vēṇṭum.
iṉi, it taṉmaiyavākiya iru kūṟṟup poruḷ kaḷuḷ, aṟivillatākiya caṭapporuḷ tāṉē iyaṅka māṭṭā tākaliṉ, ataṉai iyakkutaṟku aṟivuṭaiya piṟi toru poruḷiṉ cērkkai ataṟku iṉṟiyamaiyātu vēṇṭap paṭum. aḵtokkuñ, caṭapporuḷai iyakku taṟku aṟivuṭaiya uyirpporuḷkaḷ iruttaliṉ avaiyē pōtum,  avaṟṟiṉum vēṟākak kaṭavuḷeṉṟu vēṟō raṟivup poruḷ vēṇṭappaṭutal eṟṟukku eṉac cāṅkiya matattār kūṟupavālō veṉiṉ; avar kūṟṟum aṭātu; ēṉeṉṟāl, aṟivillāp poruḷiṉ nilaikaḷum, avaṟṟiṉ ellai kāṇappaṭāta parappukaḷum, apparappu kaḷil ellaiyillāmal aṭaṅkik kiṭakkum eṇṇiṟanta poruḷkaḷuñ ciṟṟaṟivu ciṟu toḻil kaḷuṭaiya ciṟṟuyir kaḷāl eṅṅaṉa miyakkappaṭak kūṭum? inta nilavulakattiṉ oruciṟiya viṭattilirukkum oruvaṉ, cila kāliruntu cila kālattiṉ māyntu pōkiṉṟa vaṉāyun tāṉ uyirōṭirukkuṅ kālattilum palavakai nōykaḷāṟ paṟṟappaṭṭu annōy tīrttaṟkup palavakai maruntukaḷākiya parupporuḷutaviyai vēṇṭi niṟpavaṉāyun tāṉ uyir piḻaittu vāḻtaṟku arici, paruppu, pāl, ney mutalāṉa parupporuṭ paṇṭaṅkaḷi ṉutaviyai iṉṟiyamaiyātu vēṇṭi niṟpavaṉāyun taṉ kaṇṇetirē arukilirukkum iṭaṅkaḷaiyum poruḷkaḷaiyu maṉṟittaṉ kaṭpārvaikkup puṟattē arukilirukkum poruḷkaḷaiyun taṉ kaṭpārvaik keṭṭāt tolaivi luḷḷa poruḷkaḷaiyuṅ kāṇa māṭṭātavaṉāyum, nērē arukil uḷḷa poruḷkaḷuḷḷum mikac ciṟiyaṉavā yiruppaṉavaṟṟait terintu koḷḷa māṭṭātavaṉāyum, mikap periyaṉa vāyirukkuñ cumaikaḷaiyum poruḷkaḷaiyum piṟaruṭaiya piṟa karuvikaḷi ṉuṭaiya utaviyiṉṟi acaikkamāṭṭātavaṉāyum iruttaliṉ, ivaṉatu aṟiviṟkum ivaṉatu āṟṟalukkum eṭṭāta innilavulakamākiya perum parupporuḷai ivaṉ ṟaṉṉaṟivu koṇṭun taṉ valimai koṇṭum eṅṅaṉa miyakka māṭṭuvāṉ?
parupporuḷākiya innilavulakañ ciṟitu atirn tālum itaṉkaṇ uḷḷa erimalaikaḷ veṭittu neruppaik kakkiṉālum itaṉ kaṇuḷḷa āṟuṅ kaṭalum veḷḷam perukiṉālum, itaṉkaṇ ulavuṅ kāṟṟuc cūṟāvaḷiyāyc cuḻaṉṟu aṭittālum makkaḷākiya ciṟṟuyirkaḷum, ic ciṟṟuyirkaḷā lamaikkappaṭum nāṭu nakaraṅkaḷ māṭa māḷikaikaḷum poṭipoṭiyāy noṟuṅkic cāmpalāy ventu puḻutiyāyp paṟantu aḻintu pōvatai eṅkaṇṇetirē orōvoru kālaṅkaḷil orōvōr iṭaṅkaḷiṟ kāṇkiṉṟē mallēmō? cāṉpirāṉ cikō veṉṉum amerikkāviluḷḷa māpperu nakaramum, ittāli tēyattilirunta mecīṉāp paṭṭiṉamuñ ciṟitu kālattiṟku muṉ cappāṉ tēyattil `ṭokkiyō paṭṭiṉamum nila atircciyālum erimalai neruppālum aṭiyōṭaḻintu erintu karinta kālattil aṅkaṅ kirunta makkaḷellārum paṭṭa peruntuṉpattai aṟiyā tār yār? makkaḷ avai tammait taṭukkavalla āṟṟaluṭaiya rāyiruntāl avvaḻivukaḷ nikaḻāmal taṭai ceytirukka lāmaṉṟō?
iṅṅaṉam innilavulakattiṉ orōvoru pakutikaḷil nikaḻum aḻivu nikaḻccikaḷaiyē avai nikaḻāmaltaṭai ceyyavum, aḻintu nīrilamiḻntip pōṉa nilappakutikaḷai mīṇṭummēṟ koṇarntu nilai niṟuttavuñ ciṟitum āṟṟalillāta nammakkaḷ innilavulakattait tāmākavē ākkavum aḻikkavum nilai niṟuttavum vallar āvar eṉṟu uraippatiṉum pulliyavurai vēṟuṇṭō collumiṉ! tamatuṭampu paḻutupaṭṭa viṭattu ataṉaiyē ākkik koḷḷa māṭṭāta nammaṉōr eṇṇiṟanta piṟavuyir kaḷukku eṇṇiṟanta uṭampukaḷaip paṭaittuk koṭukka vallarātalum avvuṭampukaḷoṭu kūṭiyiruntu ivvulakattu iṉpaṅkaḷai avainukarumāṟu palvēṟu paṇṭaṅkaḷai yamaittuvaikka vallarātalum avvuṭampu kaḷum ap paṇṭaṅkaḷum iruttaṟku im māpperunila vulakattaip paṭaikka vallarātalum yāṅṅaṉaṅ kūṭum? pāruṅkaḷ! innilavulakattaḷavilēyē āṟṟal mikak kuṟaintavarāy nilaiyiṉṟi uyirvāḻum nammaṉōr, innilavulakattukkum eṭṭā neṭum perun tolaivilē iṭaiveḷiyiṟ cuḻaṉṟu cellum pakalavaṉ maṇṭilam, tiṅkaḷ maṇṭilam, cevvāy maṇṭilam, putaṉ maṇṭilam, viyāḻaṉ maṇṭilam, veḷḷi maṇṭilam, caṉi maṇṭilam mutalāṉa vevvēṟu pērulakaṅkaḷaiyum ivaṟṟiṟkum .neṭun tolaivilē ivaṟṟiṉum pallāyira maṭaṅku periyaṉa vāyc cuḻaṉṟu cellum eṇṇiṟanta vāṉmīṉ maṇṭilaṅ kaḷaiyum eṅṅaṉam paṭaikkavum iyakkavum vallarāvar? ittaṉai kōṭiyulakaṅkaḷaiyuñ ciṟṟuyirkaḷākiya nām paṭaikka māṭṭuvēm, iyakka māṭṭuvēm, aḻikka māṭṭu vēm eṉṟu evarēṉuṅ kūṟa muṉ varuvarāyiṉ avarai maittuṉar palki maruntiṟ ṟeḷiyāta pittaṉeṉ ṟeḷḷic ciṟumakārum nakaiyāṭu varaṉṟō?
ākavē, ciṟṟaṟiviṉarāṉa uyirkaḷ tāmākavē māyaiyai iyakki, ataṉiliruntu tammuṭampukaḷaiyum ulakaṅkaḷaiyum ivvulakattup pala paṇṭaṅkaḷaiyum paṭaittuk koḷḷa māṭṭuvareṉṉuñ cāṅkiyarurai, piṟanta kuḻavi taṉ tāyiṉāleṭuttu aṇaikkappaṭāmal tāṉākavē pālirukkumiṭan tēṭi ataṉaip parukumeṉṟu kūṟuvāruraiyō ṭottu nakaiyāṭaṟ pālatāy muṭiyum, ātalāl, aṟivil poruḷkaḷ ellāvaṟṟiṟkum mutaṟ kāraṇamākiya māyaiyai iyakki ataṉkaṇiruntu ciṟṟuyirka ḷellāvaṟṟiṟkum avvavaṟṟiṉ iyaṟkaikku iṇaṅkap palavēṟu vakaiyāṉa uṭampukaḷaiyum avaikaḷ nukarutaṟkup palavēṟu vakaiyāṉa paṇṭaṅkaḷaiyumavaikaḷ iruntu uyirvāḻtaṟkup palvēṟu vakaiyāṉa ulakaṅkaḷaiyum muṉṉaṟintu amaittu vaittaṟku ellā aṟivum ellā āṟṟalum ellā mutaṉmaiyum ellā irakkamum oruṅkuṭaiyaṉākiya oru muḻumutaṟ kaṭavuḷ iṉṟiyamaiyātu vēṇṭappaṭuvaṉeṉpatu teḷinta muṭipāmeṉṟuṇarntu koṇmiṉkaḷ! āṉatu paṟṟiyē, irāmaliṅka aṭikaḷār epporuṭkum evvuyirkkum iṟaivaṉākiya civa pirāṉ uṇṭeṉpataṉai mēlaittiruppāṭṭil naṉ keṭuttu aṟivuṟuttaruḷiṉār
iṉi, appeṟṟiyaṉāṉa iṟaivaṉ poruḷkaḷi ṉuḷḷum puṟampum iruppaṉeṉṟu kūṟiyateṉṉai? iv viraṇṭiṉ puṟattē uruvuṭaiyaṉāy iruntē ivai tammai iyakkamāṭṭuvāṉ eṉṟu tuvitavātikaḷ kūṟutal pōlāvatu, aṟṟaṉṟu iṟaivaṉ poruḷkaḷilum uyir kaḷilum aruvāy avaṟṟiṉ akattē maṭṭum iruppāṉallatu puṟattē iruppāṉallaṉeṉa aruva vātikaḷ kūṟumuṟai pōlāvatu nuvalāmal, avai iraṇ ṭaṉakattum puṟattum iruppaṉeṉa aṭikaḷār aruḷic ceyta teṉṉaiyeṉiṟ kūṟutum; kaṭavuḷ uruvamāyē iruppa ṉeṉṟāvatu, aṉṟi aruvamāyē iruppaṉeṉ ṟāvatu colli, ellām valla avaṉatu iṟaimaik kuṇattai oru varaiyaṟaip paṭuttal avaṉṟaṉ varampilāṟṟalukku iḻukkāy muṭiyumātalāṉum, avaṉ uruva māyē iruppaṉeṉṟuraippiṉ uruvu uṭaiya ulakattup poruḷkaḷil oruvaṉāy muṭivaṉ ākalāṉum, aruva māyē niṟpaṉeṉ ṟuraippiṉ aruvamākiya vāṉ veḷi mutaliya poruḷkaḷil oṉṟāka vaittu eṇṇap paṭuvaṉākalāṉum, puṟattēiruppavaṉukku akattē ceyya vēṇṭuvaṉavum, akattē iruppavaṉukku puṟattē ceyya vēṇṭuvaṉavum aṟintu ceytal iyalāmai yāṉum, poruḷkaḷ uyirkaḷiṉ akattum puṟattum irukka vallavaṉukkē avviraṇ ṭiṭattum avaikaṭku āvaṉa aṟintu ceytal iyalu mākālāṉum, ellām valla iṟaivaṉ aruvāyum uruvāyum akattum puṟattum varai yaṟaiyiṉṟi niṟaintu niṟpaṉeṉpatē uṇmai muṭipā meṉṟu ōrntu koḷḷalvēṇṭum, ivvuṇmai tēṟṟu taṟkē āciriyar meykaṇṭa tēvanāyaṉār,
  illā mulaippāluṅ kaṇṇīrum ēntiḻaipāl
  nallāy uḷavāmāl nīrviḻal pōl - illā
  aruvāki niṉṟāṉai yāraṟivār tāṉē
  uruvākit tōṉṟāṉēl uṟṟu
eṉṟum, avartam māṇākkar aruṇanti civaṉār,
 ulakiṉiṟ patārtta mellām uruvamō ṭaruva māki
  nilaviṭum oṉṟoṉ ṟākā niṉṟaan nilaiyē pōla
  alakilā aṟivaṉ ṟāṉum aruvamē eṉṉil āyntu
  kulaviya patārttat toṉṟāyk kūṭuvaṉ kuṟit tiṭāyē
  aruvamō uruvā rūpa māṉatō aṉṟi niṉṟa
  uruvamō uraikkuṅ karttā vaṭiveṉak kuṇarttiṅ 					keṉṉiṉ
   aruvamum uruvā rūpa māṉatu maṉṟi niṉṟa
   uruvamum mūṉṟuñ coṉṉa oruvaṉuk kuḷḷa vāmē
eṉṟum, aruḷic ceytaṉarkaḷ vaṭamoḻiyil īcāvāci yōpaniṭatamum,
  avaṉaik kaṇṭu ellām naṭuṅkukiṉṟaṉa
  avaṉetaṟkum naṭuṅkuvatillai
  avaṉ ellāvaṟṟiṟkun tolaivilirukkiṉṟāṉ
  avaṉ ellāvaṟṟiṟkumarukilu mirukkiṉṟāṉ
  avaṉ ellāvaṟṟiṉ uḷḷu mirukkiṉṟāṉ
  avaṉ ellāvaṟṟiṉ veḷiyilu mirukkiṉṟāṉ
eṉṟu ivvuṇmaiyiṉaiyē naṉkeṭuttu moḻiva tāyiṟṟu.
iṉi, iṅṅaṉam poruḷkaḷiṉ uḷḷum puṟampum uyirkaḷiṉ uḷḷum puṟampum niṟaintiruppāṉāṉa iṟaivaṉiyalpu uṇarttuvāṉ pukuntu, avaṉ iyaṟkai yuṇmaiyum iyaṟkai yaṟivum iyaṟkaiyiṉpamum vāyn tāṉāki yiruppaṉeṉa aṭikaḷ moḻintiṭṭār. iṟai vaṉiyalpu ippeṟṟiyatāmeṉavē, iṟaivaṉatu utaviyai avāy niṟkum poruḷkaḷum uyirkaḷum aṅṅaṉam iyaṟkaiyilēyē uṇmaiyum aṟivum iṉpamum vāyāvā yirukku meṉpatum aṟivittārāyiṟṟu, iṉi, im mūṉṟu taṉmaikaḷiṉ ilakkaṇaṅkaḷum, poruḷkaḷum uyirkaḷum ivaitammai iyaṟkaiyilēyē yuṭaiyavākāmaiyum iṟaivaṉoruvaṉē ivaitammai iyaṟkaiyē yuṭaiyaṉā talum iṟaivaṉaic cārntē uyirkaḷ avai mūṉṟum nilaiyāka eytap peṟutalum muṟaiyē viḷakkic celvām
mutaṟkaṇ `uṇmai eṉpatu `eṉṟum orupaṭittāy niṟkum māṟānilaiyē yākum, namataṟivukkup pulaṉākum poruḷkaḷuḷ oṉṟāyiṉum māṟā nilaiyiṉatāy oru paṭittāy niṟkak kāṇkiṉṟōmā? illai illai nām uyirvāḻtaṟku iṭamāyuḷḷa immaṇṇulaku ettaṉmaiya tāyirukkiṉṟatu? oru kālattu mēṭāyiruntatu paḷḷamā kiṉṟatu. paḷḷamāyiruntatu mēṭākiṉṟatu; paṇṭaik kālattiṟ ciṟanta nākarikamuṭaiyatāy viḷaṅkiya kumari nāṭu ippōtu kaṭaluḷ amiḻntikkiṭakkiṉṟatu; kaṭaluḷ maṟaintu kiṭanta ettaṉaiyō nilappakutikaḷ ippōtu kaṭal nīrukku mēṟ pala tīvukaḷāyk kiḷampiyirukkiṉṟaṉa. ettaṉaiyō āyira āṇṭukaḷukku muṉ nākarikattiṟ ciṟanta makkaḷāl ekupti nāṭṭil amaikkappaṭṭa periyaperiya nakaraṅkaḷum annakaraṅ kaḷilamaikkappaṭṭa periyaperiya araṇmaṉaikaḷum, periya periya kōyil kaḷum, aṅṅaṉamē cālaṭi nāṭṭil `ūr eṉṉuntalainakaril eṭuppikkappaṭṭa mikap periya araṇmaṉaikaḷum, mikapperiya kōyilkaḷum, aṅṅaṉamē namatu ivvintiya nāṭṭiṉ  vaṭamēṟ pakutiyiṟ kuṭiyēṟiya paṇṭaittamiḻ makkaḷāl talainakarāka amaikkappaṭṭa `arappā eṉṉum iṭamum ataṉkaṇ irunta araṇ maṉaikaḷ kōyilkaḷum maṇmūṭuṇṭu, ippōtu mēl nāṭṭu naṉmakkaḷāl nilattiṉiṉṟum akaḻnteṭuttuk kāṭṭappaṭukiṉṟaṉa, muṉṉorukāl mēlnāṭu muḻumaiyun tam ceṅkōl nīḻalil iṉitu vaika aracu celuttiya urōma vēntarkkut talainakarā yirunta paḻaiya urōma nakariṉ mika viyakkattakka māṭa māḷikaikaḷ aḻintu pāḻāyk kiṭattalai ippōtun tiraḷ tiraḷāṉa makkaḷ ceṉṟu pārttu varukiṉṟaṉar.
iṅṅaṉamē ittamiḻ nāṭṭakattum paḻaṅkālattirunta tamiḻ vēntar amaitta araṇmaṉaikaḷun tirukkōyil kaḷum iṭintupāḻāyk kiṭattal tiruvārūr, kaṅkai koṇṭa cōḷēccuram, makāpalipuram, ceñci,vēlūr mutaliya iṭaṅkaḷil iṉṟum ellāruñ ceṉṟu pārttu varukiṉṟaṉar, vaikai mutaliya yāṟukaḷum muṟkālattu ōṭiya vaḻimāṟi ōṭukiṉṟaṉa. mikap paḻaiya nāḷil vaṭanāṭṭil ōṭiya caracuvati eṉṉumyāṟum ippōtuillāmalē pōyiṟṟu. muṉṉillāta pala yāṟukaḷ piṉṉar putiyavāyt tōṉṟi ōṭā niṟkiṉṟaṉa, nām uṟaiyum in nila maṇṭilamē varavarak kuḷirntu varukiṉṟateṉṟum, iṉṉum pallāyira nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷ kaḻintapiṉ makkaḷum piṟavuyir kaḷum uyir vāḻtaṟkē icaiyātāy itumāṟu meṉṟum nila nūl vallārkaḷperitārāyntu uraikkiṉṟaṉar,
ippōtu namakkuiravil oḷiyaittarum nilā maṇṭilamāṉatu paṉṉūṟāyira āṇṭukaḷukku muṉuyir vāḻkkaikku iṭamāyiruntu, piṉṉar varavarak kuḷirntu uyir vāḻtaṟku ēlāta iyalpiṉatāy māṟiyirukkiṉṟa teṉa vāṉnūlāciriyarkaḷ nuvalkiṉṟaṉar. iṉṉum kaṇkaḷukku ālamvittup pōlavummiṉmiṉiyoḷi pōlavun tōṉṟā niṉṟa ettaṉaiyō periya periya ulakaṅkaḷiṟ paṟpala muṉṉarē aḻintupaṭṭaṉavāyum vēṟu pala putitu tōṉṟiṉa vāyum, maṟṟum pala aḻintoḻiyum nilaimaiyil iruppaṉa vāyum paṟpala māṟutalkaṭku uṭpaṭṭaṉavāyc celkiṉṟaṉa veṉṉum uṇmai vāṉnūl ārāycciyāṟ pulaṉāyk koṇṭirukkiṉṟatu, nām nukarum paṇṭaṅkaḷ nām puḻaṅkum ēṉaṅkaḷ mutalāyiṉavellām nāḷēṟa nāḷēṟat tamatukaṭṭuc citaintun tēyntum aḻintu pōtal pōlavum, namakku neruṅkiya toṭarpuṭaiya nammuṭampum nāḷēṟa nāḷēṟa narai tirai mūppup piṇikaḷ uṭaiyavākik kaṭṭut taḷarntu nammaiviṭṭu aḻitalpōlavum, ivvulakamum ivvulakattaic cūḻnta eṇṇiṟanta ulakaṅkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ kaṇ uḷḷa aḷavilāp paṇṭaṅkaḷum oru kāṟṟōṉṟic cilakāl iruntu piṟitorukāṟ kaṭṭuk kulaintu kāṇāmaṟ pōy viṭukiṉṟaṉa, ākavē, mikac ciṟiyatumutal mikap periya tīṟāṉa ellā vakaiyāṉa uyirillāp poruḷkaḷum orē nilaiyāka irukka māṭṭātaṉavāyc citaintoḻintu pōtalāl, avaikaḷai orē nilaiyiluḷḷa uṇmaip poruḷkaḷākak kūṟutal iyalātu.
iṉi, uyiruṭaiya poruḷkaḷiṉ nilai ciṟitu ārāyaṟpāṟṟu, uyiruṭaiyavaikaḷ ellām aṟivuṭai yaṉavāy iruntum, avaṟṟiṉaṟivu eṉṟum orē taṉmaiya tākac curuṅkutal viritaliṉṟi eṉṟum orē aḷaviṉatāka, akaviruḷāl iṭaiyiṭaiyē paṟṟappaṭāta ṉavāka viḷaṅkak kaṇṭatuṇṭō? nāmciṟu makavāyirunta ñāṉṟu namataṟivu ennilaiyilirunta teṉpatu namakkē viḷaṅkavillai, āṉālum, ettaṉaiyō ciṟu makavukaḷai nām nam kaṇṇetirē kaṇṭu, avaṟṟiṉ iyalpai uṟṟunōkkutal koṇṭu, avaṟṟiṉ aṟivu ētō oru pēriruḷāṟ kavarappaṭṭirukkiṉṟateṉṟun tāy tantaiyar uṭaṉ piṟantār nēyar āciriyar mutalāṉa piṟa aṟivuṭai yuyirkaḷiṉ cērkkaiyiliruntu ammakavukaḷ ciṟituciṟitāka avviruḷ nīṅki aṟivu viḷaṅkap peṟṟu varukiṉṟaṉa veṉṟun terintu koḷkiṉṟō mātalāl, nāmuñ ciṟu kuḻavikaḷāy irunta ñāṉṟu ēṉaik kuḻavikaḷaip pōlavē nammai iṉṉāreṉṟum, nammaic cūḻntavar iṉṉāreṉṟum, nammaic cuṟṟiyuḷḷa paṇṭaṅkaḷ iṉṉavaiyeṉṟum uṇaramāṭṭātavarkaḷāyiruntōm eṉpatai aiyamaṟat teḷintu koḷkiṉṟōm.
itu maṭṭumō nām āṇṭil mutirntavarkaḷāyk kalvippayiṟciyāṟ, pēraṟiñar cērkkaiyāl aṟivu viḷaṅkappeṟṟa piṉṉum, namataṟivu orutaṉmaittāy viḷaṅkuvatiṉṟip palvēṟu māṟutalkaḷiṉ vayappaṭṭu, nammai iṭaiyiṭaiyē tuṉpuṟutti varak kāṇkiṉṟēm allēmō? nēṟṟu aṟintavaikaḷiṟ ceṉṟakiḻamaiyil aṟintavaikaḷiṟ, pōṉa tiṅkaḷil aṟintavaikaḷiṟ, kaṭanta āṇṭu aṟintavaikaḷil ettaṉai kōṭi niṉaivukaḷ ettaṉaikōṭi eṇṇaṅkaḷ nammaṟivukkut teṉ paṭāmalē maṟaintu pōyiṉa! muṉṉaṟintavaikaḷai iṅṅaṉam nām maṟantu maṟantupōtalāl namakku varum iṭarkaḷukku, nammāṟ piṟarkku varumiṭarkaḷukku oru kaṇakkuṇṭō! muṉṉaṟinta vaikaḷai maṟattalāṟ, nikaḻntavaikaḷai nikaḻntapaṭiyē colla māṭṭātavarkaḷāyp pārtta poruḷ kēṭṭa poruḷ cuvaitta poruḷ iyalpukaḷai appārtta kēṭṭa cuvaitta paṭiyē niṉaivu kūrntu collamāṭṭāta varkaḷāy poyttup piḻaipaṭukiṉṟaṉam allamō?
kaṭantavaṟṟaiyaṟiyum aṟiviṟṟāṉ ittakaiya māṟutalkaḷ nikaḻkiṉṟaṉa veṉṟālum, ippōtu nam kaṇṇetirē nikaḻvaṉavaṟṟilāvatu namataṟivu poypaṭu taliṉṟi meyyāka aṟital ceykiṉṟatā? atuvum illaiyē! nām nērē kāṇkiṉṟa poruḷaiyum ārāyntu pārttālaṉṟi aiyamaṟat teḷiyamāṭṭātavar kaḷāy irukkiṉṟōm, orutuṇṭu cempaiyum orutuṇṭu poṉṉaiyum paḷicceṉṟu tōṉṟa viḷakkik kaiyiṟ koṭuttāl, avai yiraṇṭaṟkumuḷḷa vēṟu pāṭu teriyāmal ettaṉai pēr ēmāntu pōkiṉṟaṉar! tittippāṉa nārattampaḻaṅ koṭi muntirippaḻam mutaliyaṉa vāṅkupavaril ettaṉaipēr avaṟṟai naṉku āyntu pārāmal vāṅkittām vāṅkiyavai tittippukku māṟāṉa puḷippuṭaiyavāyiruttalaik kaṇṭu ēmāntu pōkiṉṟaṉar. maruttuvaril ettaṉaipēr nōyāḷikaḷiṉ nōyiṉ ṟaṉmaiyai yaṟiyāmal māṟāṉa maruntukaḷaik koṭuttu avar koṇṭa nōyai mikuttu avaraiyuṅ kollu kiṉṟaṉar! kaṇakkaril ettaṉaipēr tavaṟākak kaṇakkuc ceytutāmum piḻaipaṭṭut. tām cārnta celvaraiyuṅ keṭuttuviṭukiṉṟaṉar! camaiyaṟ ṟoḻil ceyvāril ettaṉaipēr kālamumiṭamum uṇaveṭuppār uṭal nilaiyum āyntu pārāmal uṇavu camaittuk koṭuttu, ataṉaiyuṇpārkkup pala koṭu nōykaḷai yellām varuvittu viṭukiṉṟaṉar! nūl eḻutuvāril ettaṉaipēr kālappōkku makkaḷ muṉṉēṟṟaṅ karutip pārāmaṟ payaṉaṟṟa poruḷkaḷai eḻuti makkaḷaṟivu vaḷarcciyaip pāḻpaṭuttu kiṉṟaṉar! avaruḷ ettaṉaipēr tām vaḻaṅkum moḻiyait tiruttamākap payilāmaṟ coṟkuṟṟañ coṟṟoṭark kuṟṟam potuḷa eḻutiyum, ayaṉmoḻic coṟkaḷai aṭut taṭuttuk kalantum ataṉ tūya vaḻakkai māya vaikkiṉṟaṉar? iṉṉum iṅṅaṉamē nam makkaḷiṟ perumpālār - avar kaṟṟavarāyiṉuṅ kallātavarāyiṉun, taṅkaṇṇetirē kāṇpaṉavuñ ceyvaṉavumellām uṇmaikku māṟākap piḻaipaṭac ceytutān tuṉpuṟuva toṭu piṟaraiyun tuṉpuṟac ceykiṉṟaṉar! ivvāṟu ivarkaḷ nikaḻkāla nikaḻcci kaḷilēyum meyyoṭu tiṟampip poypaṭumaṟiviṉa rāyirukkak kāṇṭaliṉ, ivarkaḷatu aṟivai uṇmai nilaiyiṉateṉ ṟuraikka meyyaṟivu vāyntār oruppaṭuvarō coṉmiṉkaḷ!
iṉi, iṅṅaṉaṅ kaṭanta kāla nikaḻccikaḷilum nikaḻ kāla nikaḻccikaḷilum aṭuttaṭuttup piḻaipaṭum aṟivuṭaiya ivarkaḷ,pulaṉuṇarvu maṉaṉuṇarvukku eṭṭāta varuṅkāla nikaḻccikaḷai muṉṉaṟintu naṭattal yāṅṅaṉaṅ kaikūṭum? nām neṭuṅkālam uyirvāḻntu ivvulaka iṉpaṅkaḷait tuyppōmeṉṟu eṇṇik koṇṭa ettaṉai pēr ēḻaiyeḷiyavarkaḷ vayiṟṟai yoṭukkic cērtta poruḷai avar kaṇṇīr cintak kaippaṟṟik koṇṭu vanta annērat tilēyē caṭutiyil uyir tuṟantu piṇamāyk kiṭakkak kaṇṭatillaiyā? tamakku mēl valimaiyuṭaiyavar kaḷillai yeṉṟuiṟumāntu valiyaṟṟavarkaḷait tuṉpuṟutti vantavarkaḷ tām tammiṉum valiyārāl veṭṭappaṭṭu vīḻntu kiṭakkavillaiyā? ettaṉai celvarkaḷ ettaṉai aracarkaḷ ettaṉai maṟavarkaḷ tamatu nilaiyāmaiyai uṇarntu pārāmaiyāl, nāḷaikku namatu nilaimai yeppaṭiyāmō eṉṟu aiyuṟavu koḷḷāmaṟ piṟarkkut tīṅkiḻaittup piṉṉarttām irunta iṭamun teriyāmal iṟantu pōkiṉṟaṉar, varuṅkāla viyalpai uṇarntu pārkka māṭṭāta nammakkaḷaṟivum aṟiveṉap paṭumā? taṉ uṭampiṉuḷḷun taṉṉaiccuṟṟilum nikaḻap pōvaṉa vaṟṟaiyē yaṟiya māṭṭātāṉ oruvaṉ, tāṉ oruvaṉē pallāṇṭu nīṭu vāḻvaṉ ēṉaiyō rellām nīṭuvāḻār eṉa niṉaintu piṟarkku muṉ tāṉēmāyntu ēmāṟṟam aṭaivaṉāyiṟ ciṟṟaṟivuṭaiya immakaṉaṟivu uṇmaiyai yuḷḷapaṭi yuṇaravalla uṇmaiyuṭaiyatāmō? makkaḷiṉ ic ciṟṟaṟivup peṟṟi tēṟṟutaṟ kaṉṟō āciriyar meykaṇṭa tēva nāyaṉār,
aṟintum aṟivatē yāyumaṟiyā
taṟintataiyum viṭṭaṅ kaṭaṅki - aṟinta
tetuvaṟivum aṉṟākum meykaṇṭāṉ oṉṟiṉ
atuvatutāṉ eṉṉumakam
eṉṟu aruḷic ceytār, piṟ kālattil tāyumāṉa aṭikaḷum,
iṉiyē temakkuṉ aruḷvarumō veṉakkaruti
	ēṅkuteṉ neñcam aiyō
iṉṟaik kiruntārai nāḷaikkiruppar eṉṟu
	eṇṇavō tiṭam illaiyē
eṉṟu aruḷic ceytamai kāṇmiṉkaḷ! iṅṅaṉa mellāñ celkāla, nikaḻkāla, varuṅkāla nilaikaḷai uṇarum uṇmai yaṟivu vāyāmaiyāl,makkaḷum maṟṟai yuyirkaḷum iyaṟkai yuṇmai nilaiyuṭaiya vākāmai teṟṟeṉa viḷaṅkāniṟkum,.
itukāṟum ārāyntavāṟṟāl uyiril poruḷ kaḷum uyiruḷ poruḷkaḷum iyaṟkaiyilēyē uṇmai nilai yuṭaiyavallāmainaṉku peṟappaṭutalāl, uyirkaḷ tāmuntammai ōr aṟivuṭaip poruḷeṉṟu tamatuṇmai uṇartaṟkun tamakku uṭampumulakamum ulakattup pal poruḷkaḷumākat tiripeytip payaṉpaṭum māyaiyeṉṟu oru poruḷ uṇṭu eṉṟu ataṉ uṇmaiyiṉai avaikaḷ aṟitaṟkuṅ, kaṭavuḷ eṉṟu oru muḻumutaṟporuḷ eṉṟum māṟā iyaṟkai uṇmaiyuṭaiyatāy avai iraṇṭaṉai yun toṭarpu paṭuttutaṟku vēṇṭumeṉpatu iṉṟiyamai yātu peṟappaṭum, eṉṉai? taṉṉilē māṟā uṇmai nilaiyillā oruvaṉ piṟarkku ētoru naṉmaiyuñ ceyya māṭṭāmai namatu ulaka vāḻkkaiyil aṭuttaṭuttu nikaḻakkāṇṭum aṉṟō? taṉ maṉakkiṉiya naṇpaṉ paṭṭa kaṭaṉai nāḷai vantutīrttu avaṉaic ciṟaipukāmaṟ kāppēṉ eṉṟu uṟuti collip pōṉa oru celvaṉ tāṉ coṉṉa moḻiyun taṉ niṉaivum māṟiviṭuvaṉā yiṉ, avaṉ avaṟku avvutaviyaic ceyyā toḻital pōlavum, veppu nōyāl varuntu moruvaṉukku annōy tīrkka vanta maruttuvaṉ oruvaṉ nāḷai viḻumiya toru maruntu koṇarntūṭṭi ataṉait tīrppēṉeṉac collip pōy maṟunāḷil aṅṅaṉamē vantu ceyyā toḻiyiṉ avaṉ avaṟkuc ciṟitum payaṉpaṭāmai pōlavum, oru koṭiyaṉāl tuṉpuṟuttappaṭṭu naṭunilai maṉṟattil muṟaiyiṭṭāṉ oruvaṉukku muṟai ceyyap pukunta oru naṭuvaṉ taṉkaṭamaiyiṉiṉṟum vaḻīit, tuṉpuṟuttiya koṭiyaṉpāṟ kaikkūli vāṅkik koṇṭu muṟai ceyyātu māṟuvaṉāyiṉ avaṉ taṉpāl muṟai vēṇṭiṉāṉukku muṟai ceyyātu kuṟai ceytal pōlavum iṟaivaṉum māṟuntakaiyaṉāyiṉ, avaṉ uyir kaḷukku ētōru naṉmaiyuñ ceyya māṭṭānilai yiṉaṉāy viṭuvaṉ.
maṟṟu, avaṉ eṇṇiṟanta vuyirkaḷukkum avvavaṟ ṟiṉ aṟivu nilaikkēṟpa eṇṇiṟanta uṭampukaḷaik koṭuttu avaṟṟiṉ aṟivai mēṉmēl viḷaṅkac ceytu varutalaiyum, avaṟṟiṉ aṟivu viḷakkattiṟku iṉṟi yamaiyāta veyil maṇṭila, nila maṇṭila iyakkaṅkaḷaic ciṟitum māṟāmal nikaḻac ceytu varutalaiyum avaṟṟiṉ uyir vāḻkkaikku iṉṟiyamaiyāta puṟpūṇṭumarañ ceṭi koṭikaḷai māṟāmal viḷaivittu avai kāy, kaṉi, kiḻaṅku, vittuk, kīrai mutalāṉa payaṉkaḷait toṭar pākat tarumāṟu utavi varutalaiyum maḻai peyyuṅ kālattu maḻai peyyavum ataṟkuk karuviyāṉa kuḷirṅkāṟṟu vēṇṭuṅ kālattu ak kuḷirṅkāṟṟu vīcavum, maḻaikku mutalāṉa nīrāvi eḻutaṟku veyil mikuṅkālattu veyil mikuntu eṟippavum māṟāmaṟ purintu varutalaiyum, ivvāṟṟāl ellāvuyirkaḷum uṇavu peṟṟu avaṟṟai yuṭ koṇṭa piṉ akattiruntu avvuṇavaic cāṟuñ cakkaiyumākap pirittuc cāṟṟaic cennīrāka māṟṟi uṭampeṅkum ōṭac ceytuñ cakkaiyai uṭampiṉiṉṟu uṭaṉukkuṭaṉ kaḻiyac ceytum nam perumāṉ oru noṭip poḻutum maṭintirātupērutavi yāṟṟi varutalaiyum, avaṉ amaitta māṟānikaḻccikaḷukku māṟupaṭāmal namatu vāḻkkaiyiṉai nām celuttat teriyāmal aṟiyāmai yiṉālum iṟumāppiṉālum tīya iṉpa vēṭkaiyiṉālum namatu uṭampai nāmē citaittu viṭṭa viṭattu ataṉai yoḻittunamakku vēṟu putiya uṭampukaḷaik koṭuttu nammai vēṟu vēṟu putiya piṟavikaḷil uyttu varutalaiyum nām naṉkārāyntu āḻntu niṉaiyuṅkāl, ellām valla nam āṇṭavaṉ oruvaṉē ekkālattum evviṭattum māṟā, uṇmainilai yuṭaiyaṉā yirukkiṉṟaṉa ṉeṉpatūum, avaṉ oruvaṉē ellā uyirkaḷiṭattum - avai ettuṇai iḻintaṉa vāyiṉum ettuṇai uyarntavaṉavāyiṉum, avaṟṟiṉ pālellām māṟā aṉpiṉaṉāy māṟā aruḷiṉaṉāy irukkiṉṟaṉa ṉeṉpatūum uḷḷaṅkai nellikkaṉip pōṟ ṟeḷḷat teḷiya viḷaṅkukiṉṟaṉa vallavō? ākaiyāl, iyaṟkai yuṇmai vāyntavaṉ iṟaivaṉ oruvaṉē yallāmal maṟṟaiya allaveṉṟu mēlaittiruppāṭṭil aṭikaḷār aṟivuṟuttiṉamai cālap porutta muṭaittātal kaṇṭu koḷḷappaṭum,
iṉi, iv vulakattiṉkaṇ uḷḷa ciṟṟuyirkaḷellām orē taṉmaittāka māṟātuviḷaṅkumaṟivu vāyntavai allaveṉpatu muṉṉarē kāṭṭappaṭṭamaiyāl av vuyirkaḷellāvaṟṟiṟkum vēṇṭuvaṉavellāmaṟintu utavi ceytaṟku eṉṟum oru peṟṟittāy vayaṅkāniṉṟa pēraṟivu vāynta mutalvaṉ oruvaṉ iṉṟiyamaiyātu vēṇṭappaṭuvaṉeṉpatu muṭikkappaṭum. ettaṉaiyō uyirkaḷ putitu tōṉṟuvaṉavāyum ettaṉaiyō uyirkaḷ iṟantu paṭupavaṉavāyumettaṉaiyō uyirkaḷ vēṟu piṟavikaḷiṟ celvaṉavāyum uyirkaḷiṉ tōṟṟakkēṭukaḷ iṭai yaṟātu eṅkumnikaḻak kāṇṭaliṉ, avvavvuyir kaḷiṉ akattum puṟattumiruntu avvavvaṟṟiṉ tōṟṟak kēṭukaḷai yaṟintu avvavvaṟṟiṟkup piṟarevarāluñ ceyya muṭiyāta pērutaviyaic ceyyum perumāṉ aṟivu, ettuṇaip periyatāy ettuṇai viḻumiya viḷakkat takkatāy ettuṇai aruḷirakkam vāyntatāy ettuṇaic ciṟantamāṟā nilaiyiṉatāy miḷirvatākal vēṇṭum! pacittu uṇavu vēṇṭi vantārkku uṇavu koṭukkum oruvaṉ, ōriṭattil orukālattuḷḷār cilarkku maṭṭumē uṇavu koṭukka vallaṉāvaṉ; teṟkē kumariyiṟ pacitta palarkkum, iṭaiyē ceṉṉai vēṅkaṭam mutalāṉa ūrkaḷiṟ pacittuḷḷār palarkkum, vaṭakkē kāci avanti mutalāṉa ūrkaḷiṟ pacittār palarkkum avaṉ oruvaṉē orē kālattil uṇavaḷikka māṭṭuvaṉō? māṭṭāṉaṉṟē? oṉṟai viṭṭu oṉṟu mika akaṉṟirukkum avvavvūr kaḷiluḷḷa vaṟiñarkkellām aṅṅaṉamavaṉ orē kālattil uṇavaḷikka vēṇṭiṉ, āṅkāṅku uṇavamaip pār paṟpalarai niṟuvip perum poruḷ celaviṭṭu avar vāyilāka aṅṅaṉañ ceyyalāmēyaṉṟittāṉ oruva ṉākavē yiruntuaṅṅaṉañ ceyyamāṭṭuvāṉ allaṉ!
iṅṅaṉamē nōykoṇṭu varuntuvārkku nōy tīrkku maruttuvaṉum, orukālattu ōriṭattuḷḷa oru cilarkku maṭṭumē nōy tīrkkamāṭṭuvāṉaṉṟi, oṉṟiṉoṉṟu akaṉṟirukkum paṟpala ūriluḷḷa paṟpalarkkum orē kālattil nōy nīkkamāṭṭuvaṉallaṉ; aṉṟi avaṉ aṅṅaṉañ ceyyavēṇṭiṟ perum poruḷ celavu ceytu maruttuvar paṟpalarai āṅkāṅku ēviyē nōy koṇṭār pallāyiravarkkum maruntūṭṭi nōy tīrkkaṟ pālaṉ, maṟṟu, ellām valla, kaṭavuḷō ōriṭattaṉṟi ōrūrilaṉṟi ōrulakattaṉṟip palvēṟiṭaṅkaḷilum palvēṟu ūrkaḷilum palvēṟu ulakaṅkaḷilum niṟaintu āṟṟu maṇalilum aḷaviṭappaṭātaṉavāy uḷḷa uyirt tokaiḷil ettaṉai kōṭiyuyirkaḷai orē kālattil tōṟṟu vikkiṉṟāṉ, ettaṉai kōṭi yuyirkaḷai orē kālattiṟ kāttu varukiṉṟāṉ, ettaṉai kōṭiyuyirkaḷai orē kālattil oru piṟaviyiṉiṉṟu viṭuvittu vēṟu pala piṟavikaḷil uyttu varukiṉṟāṉ, ettaṉai kōṭi yuyirkaḷai orē kālattil taṉ tiruvaṭippēriṉpattiṟ paṭivittuvarukiṉṟāṉ!
iṅṅaṉamāka ellāiṭaṅkaḷilum ellāk kālaṅ kaḷilun tāṉ oruvaṉākavē yiruntu, ellā uyirkaḷiṉ iyalpukaḷaiyum aṟintu avvavaṟṟiṟkēṟṟa utavikaḷait tāṉ oruvaṉākavē ceytuvarum iṟaivaṉṟaṉ aṟi vāṟṟal evvaḷavu  ciṟantatāy, evvaḷavu pēraḷa viṉatāy irukka vēṇṭu meṉpataic ciṟitu eṇṇip pāruṅkaḷ! ittuṇaic ciṟantaavaṉaṟivu, nammaṉōrk kuḷḷa aṟivupōl āṇava valliruḷil maṟaintu iruntu, piṉ ataṉiṉṟum viṭupaṭṭu viḷaṅkuvatāyirukkakkūṭumō eṉpataiyum eṇṇip pāruṅkaḷ! avaṉaṟivu ciṟitu maṟaintiruntāl ivvulaka meṅkē, ivvulakattuḷḷa paṇṭaṅkaḷeṅkē, puṟ pūṇṭukaḷ eṅkē, palvakaip piṟavikaḷeṅkē, palvakai yuyirkaḷeṅkē, ñāyiṟu tiṅkaḷeṅkē, ellām veṟum pāḻāy veṭṭa veḷiyā yirukkumallavō? ātalāl ellām valla iṟaivaṉaṟivu, namataṟivu pōl āṇava valliruḷiṟ ciṟitum maṟaiyātāy, eṉṟum viḷaṅkiṉa paṭiyāyē yirukkum eṉpatu ciṟitum aiyamiṉṟit teḷiyaṟpālatākum, itupaṟṟiyē aṭikaḷār avaṉ iyaṟkai yaṟiviṉaṉāyē yiruppaṉ eṉṟu mēlait tiruppāṭṭil aruḷic ceytāreṉ ṟuṇarntu koḷḷal vēṇṭum.
iṉi, iṟaivaṉ iyaṟkaiyuṇmaiyiṉaṉāyum iyaṟkai yaṟiviṉaṉāyummaṭṭumē iruppaṉeṉṟu uraip piṉ, ivviraṇṭu ilakkaṇaṅkaḷum māyai āṇavaṅ kaḷukkum, māyai āṇavaṅkaḷiṉiṉṟum viṭupaṭṭa uyir kaḷukkum muṟaiyē poruntuvāṉ cellum, eṉṉai?  uṭampāyum uṭampiṉ karuvikaḷāyum ulakaṅkaḷāyum ulakattup poruḷkaḷāyun tiripuṟāta māyaiyiṉ parappu iṉṉum evvaḷavō peritāy irukkiṉṟatu!  innāl vakaik kāriyap poruḷkaḷāyt tirinta māyaiyiṉ pakuti ciṟitāyum, aṅṅaṉan tiriyāta pakuti peritāyum, uḷa veṉpatu ārāycciyāl naṉku pulaṉātaliṉ, tiripillāta ataṉ mutaṟkāraṇap pakuti iyaṟkai yuṇmai nilai yuṭaiyatātal tāṉē peṟappaṭu maṉṟō? iṅṅaṉamē, piṟavikku vārāta uyirkaḷum iṉṉum ettaṉaiyō kōṭik kaṇakkāy uḷḷaṉavākaliṉ avai tammaip paṟṟiyirukkum āṇavamalamuṅ kāriyap paṭātatāy iyaṟkai yuṇmainilai vāyntatātalun tāṉē peṟappaṭumaṉṟō?  ivai pōlavē catā civa tattuvattil vaikuñ catācivar mutalāyiṉār iyaṟkaiyilēyē āṇavamalap paṟṟup peritum akaṉṟavarāy, iyalpākavē pēraṟivu viḷakkam vāyntavarāy iruttaliṉ, aṉṉārkkuḷḷa avvaṟivu iyaṟkaiyē yuṇmai nilaiyuṭaiyatātalun tāṉē peṟappaṭum; peṟappaṭavē iṟaivaṉ oruvaṉē iyaṟkai yuṇmaiyiṉaṉāyum iyaṟkaiyaṟiviṉaṉāyum viḷaṅkuva ṉeṉṟa coṟ kuṟṟamuṭaittāy muṭiyumām piṟaveṉiṉ; aṟṟaṉṟu, akkuṟṟam aṇukāmaip poruṭṭē iyal uṇmai  yaṟivu eṉṟavaḷavil amaiyātu, iṉpam eṉpa toṉṟu kūṭṭi, iyal uṇmai aṟivu iṉpavaṭivāki eṉṟu ōti, iṟaivaṉ varampiliṉpa vaṭiviṉa ṉāy niṟṟal avaṟkuc ciṟanta ilakkaṇamātal aṭikaḷ pulappaṭa vaittār.
aḵtokkumā ṟeṉṉai? māyaiyiṟ ṟiraṇṭa paṇṭaṅkaḷiṟ pala iṉ cuvaiyiṉavāyum, vēṟupala naṟumaṇattiṉa vāyum, maṟṟum pala iṉṉōcaiyiṉavāyum, iṉṉum pala aḻakiya tōṟṟattiṉavāyum, piṉṉum pala iṉiya meṉmaiyiṉavāyum, pukuntu uyirkaḷ pāl iṉpattai viḷaikkak kāṇṭaliṉ, māyaiyum iṉpa muṭaitteṉpatu pōtarumālō veṉiṉ aḵtovvātu; eṉṉai? māyaiyiṟ ṟiraṇṭa paṇṭaṅkaḷ aṟivuṭaiya uyirkaḷ māṭṭuiṉpa tuṉpaṅkaḷai viḷaivittaṟkuk karuviyā mallatu, avaitāmē iṉpa tuṉpaṅkaḷ uṭaiyavallavā kaliṉ, māyai aṟivil poruḷātal kaṇ kūṭāka aṟiyak kiṭattaliṉ, atutāṉē taṉakku iṉpa muṇṭeṉṟātal tuṉpamuṇṭeṉṟātal aṟital ceyyātu, kaṉinta palāccuḷaiyait tēṉōṭu kūṭṭi yuṇṭavaṉukku ataṉ iṉcuvaiyiṉāl iṉpam mītūrak kāṇṭumēyaṉṟi, appalāccuḷaiyē avviṉ cuvai iṉpattai eytak kāṇṭumillaiyē, oru kūrṅ kattiyāṟ kai yaṟuppuṇṭa vaṉukku ataṉāl tuṉpammītūrak kāṇṭumē yaṉṟi, akkattiyē avaṉ kaiyai aṟuttu ataṉāl tuṉpuṟak kāṇṭumillaiyē ivvāṟē iṉpa tuṉpa nikaḻccikaḷ aṟivuṭaiya vuyirkaḷ pālaṉṟi, avai tammai avaṟṟiṉ pāl viḷaivikkum māyaiyiṉ kāriyaṅkaḷil oru ciṟituṅ kāṇkilamākaliṉ,māyai avaṟṟiṟkuk karuviyēyā mallāmal,avai tāmē iṉpa tuṉpaṅkaḷai uṭaiyavākā eṉpatu ellārkkum oppa muṭinta uṇmaiyē yām,
aḵtokkumāyiṉum, aṟivuṭaiya uyirkaḷ iṉpam eytak kāṇṭaliṉ, avaiyum iṉpamuṭaitteṉpatu peṟap paṭum; paṭavē, iṟaivaṉ oruvaṉē iṉpa muṭaiyavaṉ eṉṟu uraittatu iḻukkāmālō veṉiṉ; iḻukkākātu; eṉṉai? uyirkaḷellām māyaiyiṟ ṟiraṇṭa paṇṭaṅ kaḷai nukartalāṉum aḻaku aṟivu aṉpuṭaiya piṟavuyir kaḷoṭu cērtalāṉumē iṉpuṟak kāṇṭumallatu, avviraṇṭaṉ ṟoṭarpiṉṟit tāṉē taṉittiruntu iṉpuṟum oruciṟṟuyirai yāṇṭuṅ kaṇṭila mākaliṉ eṉpatu iṅṅaṉampiṟitoṉṟu iṉpuṟuttiṉālaṉṟi iṉpuṟal aṟiyāta ciṟṟuyirkaḷai iyaṟkaiyiṉpamuṭaiyaṉa veṉṟāl icaiyāmaiyiṉ muḻumutaṟ kaṭavuḷoruvaṉē iyaṟkai yiṉpamuṭaiya ṉeṉpatu muṭikkappaṭum; paṭavē, avaṟkuriya acciṟappilakkaṇam piṟitetaṟkum urimaiyā vāṉ cēṟal illaiyeṉṟu kaṭaippiṭittuṇarntu koḷḷal vēṇṭum.
atuvēyu maṉṟic ciṟṟuyirkaḷellān tuṉpuṟuttum poruṭ cērkkaiyāl tuṉpuṟṟum, iṉ puṟuttum poruṭ cērkkaiyāl iṉpuṟṟum varutal pōliṟaivaṉ etaṉālēṉun tuṉpuṟuttappaṭutalum piṟitetaṉālēṉum iṉpuṟuttappaṭutalum illaṉāy, eññāṉṟum iṉpa vuruviṉa ṉāyē niṟkum peṟṟiyaṉākaliṉ, avaṟkuriya acciṟappilakkaṇam piṟitetaṟkum uriyatātal cellā teṉṟu uṇartal vēṇṭum, iṟaivaṉ iṅṅaṉam iṉpa vuruviṉaṉāyē niṟkun taṉip pēruṇmai aṟivuṟuttu taṟkaṉṟō māṇikka vācakap perumāṉ,
cōtiyāyt tōṉṟum uruvamē aruvām
	oruvaṉē collutaṟkariya
ātiyē naṭuvē antamē pantam
	aṟukkum āṉantamāk kaṭalē
eṉṟum,
ōriṉpaveḷḷattu urukkoṇṭu toṇṭarai
yuḷḷaṅ koṇṭār
eṉṟum, iṅṅaṉamē irāmaliṅka aṭikaḷār, makātēva mālaiyil,
taṟpōta voḻiviṉiṭai niṟaintu poṅkit
	tatumpivaḻin tōṅkiyelān tāṉē yākic
ciṟpōtat takampuṟamuṅ kōttuniṉṟa
	civāṉantap perukkēmeyc celvattēvē
eṉṟum aruḷic ceytiruttalum niṉaiviṟ patikkaṟ pāṟṟu
aṟṟēl, iṟaivaṉōṭotta aṟiviṉarāka ōtap paṭuñ catācivar mutalāyiṉār māṭṭum iyaṟkaiyiṉpam uḷatākal vēṇṭumā leṉiṉ; aṟṟaṉṟu, catācivar mutaliyōrkkut talaimai celuttutalāl uṇṭākuñ cerukkākiya `atikāramalam oṉṟuṇṭeṉa nūlkaḷ ōtutaliṉ, avaratu aṟivuñ ciṟitu kuṟaipāṭuṭaiyatē yākum; aḵtuṭaiya tākavē, avvaṟivaip paṟṟi nikaḻum iṉpamuṅ kuṟaipāṭuṭai yatākavē yirukkum,ataṉāl, avarkkuḷḷa ak kuṟaipāṭu nīṅkutaṟkum, avar ellām valla muḻumutaṟ civattiṉ utaviyaiyē avāy niṟṟaliṉ, avarumac civattiṉ ṟiruvaruṭpēriṉpattait talaikkūṭi, ataṉkaṭ paṭinta piṉṉarē niṟainta aṟivumniṟainta iṉpamum uṭaiyarāva reṉpatu muṭikkappaṭum,aṅṅaṉam muṭikkappaṭavē, muppattāṟu parupporuḷ nuṇporuṭ paṭikaḷukkum, avvappaṭikaḷil vaikuvārāy mummala irumala orumalap paṟṟuṭaiya mūvakai yāruyirt tokuti kaḷiṉ nilaikaḷukkum appāṟpaṭṭa iyalpiṉatāyuḷḷa civam oṉṟē iyaṟkai yuṇmaiyum iyaṟkai yaṟivum iyaṟkai yiṉpamum uṭaiyatāy, ellāvaṟṟaiyun taṉ aruḷ veḷiyilaṭakki avaṟṟiṉ mēlum ellaiyillāta viriviṉatāy viḷaṅkumeṉpatum muṭikkappaṭum,
aḵtokkuṅ, kāriyappaṭāta māyaiyiṉ perum pakutiiyaṟkai yuṇmai yuṭaiyatātal muṉṉarē peṟap paṭṭamaiyiṉ, aḵtu avvāṟṟāl iṟaivaṉiyalpōṭu ottatāvāṉ cellumā leṉiṉ; māyaiyiṉ oru pakuti viyaṟkaiyuṇmaiyuṭaiyatāyiṉum ataṉ maṟṟoru pakuti iyaṟkaiyuṇmai vāyātāyk kāriyappaṭṭu māṟak kāṇṭaliṉālum, aṅṅaṉan taṉṉilē iruvēṟu vakaippaṭṭu niṟkum apporuḷ orē nilaiyiṉa teṉṟaṟku iṭam peṟāmaiyiṉālum, iṟaivaṉ atupōl aṅṅaṉan taṉṉilē iruvēṟu vakaippaṭṭu oruvakaiyiṟ kāriyamāy māṟutalum piṟitoru vakaiyiṟ kāriyamāy māṟāmal niṟṟalum ākiya vēṟupāṭu uṭaiyaṉallaṉākalāṉum māyai iṟaivaṉōṭotta uṇmai nilaiyiṉatātal cellā teṉṟu kaṭaippiṭittal vēṇṭum,
aḵtokkumaṉṉāyiṉuñ caiva cittānta nūlkaḷum iṟaivaṉukku aruvam uruvam eṉṉum iruvakai nilaikaḷum uṇṭeṉṟōtutaliṉ, avaṉum māyaipōṟ kāriyap paṭutaluṭaiya ṉeṉpatu peṟappaṭṭu, aṅṅaṉam ōtum atu māyāvātak koḷkaiyāy muṭiyumām piṟa veṉiṉ; aṟṟaṉṟu,iṟaivaṉ mācaṟṟa tūya aṟivup poruḷē yallāmal, aṟivillā māyaipōlvāṉ allaṉ, aṟivil poruḷ maṭṭumē orunilaiyiliruntu piṟitoru nilaikkut tirikkappaṭum; maṟṟu aṟivuṭaip poruḷō aṅṅaṉan tirikkappaṭutal eññāṉṟum  illai; maṇṇāṉatu kuṭamākat tirikkap paṭutal pōla, ammaṇṇiṉiṉṟu kuṭattai yuṇṭākkum aṟivuṭai yaṉāṉa kuyavaṉ aṅṅaṉan tirikkappaṭutal ilaṉ, iṉṉum, maṇṇiṉiṉṟum kuṭam mutaliya pāṇṭaṅkaḷai uṇṭākkum muṉṉarē,avai tammai yuṇṭākkuṅ kuyavaṉatu aṟiviṉkaṇ appāṇṭaṅkaḷiṉ vaṭivaṅkaḷ amaintu kiṭattal vēṇṭum;
aṅṅaṉam avvaṭivaṅkaḷ taṉ aṟiviṉkaṇ amaiyap peṟātavaṉ, appāṇṭaṅkaḷaic camaittaṟkuriya aṟivu vāyātavaṉāmākaliṉ, avaṉ avaṟṟai ākkamāṭṭuvāṉ allaṉ, ākavē, aṟivillāta māyaiyiliruntu ivvulakaṅ kaḷum ivvulakattup poruḷkaḷum ivvuṭampukaḷum iv vuṭampukaḷil amainta karuvikaḷum paṭaikkap paṭutaṟku muṉṉarēyē, ivaṟṟai yellām paṭaikkum iṟaivaṉatu pēraṟiviṉkaṇ apporuḷkaḷiṉ uruvaṅkaḷ amaintu kiṭattal vēṇṭumeṉpatu teḷiyappaṭum, eṅṅaṉaṅ kuyavaṉatuaṟiviṉkaṇ amainta uruvaṅkaḷōṭu otta vaṭivuṭaiya pāṇṭaṅkaḷ maṇṇiṉiṉṟumavaṉāṟ paṭaikkappaṭukiṉṟaṉavō, aṅṅaṉamē iṟaivaṉatu aṟiviṉkaṇ amainta uruvaṅkaḷōṭotta vaṭivuṭaiya ulakamum uṭampumpiṟavum māyaiyiṉiṉṟum avaṉāṟ paṭaikkappaṭuvaṉavākum, eṅṅaṉaṅ kuṭaṅkaḷ mutalāṉa pāṇṭaṅkaḷiṉ vaṭivu citaintaḻintālumavaṟṟō ṭotta aṟiviṉuruvaṅkaḷ kuyavaṉuḷḷattiṉkaṇ aḻiyā vāy niṉṟu ap pāṇṭaṅkaḷai maṟittummaṟittun tōṟṟu vikkumō, aṅṅaṉamē ivvulakaṅkaḷumiv vuṭampukaḷum piṟavum vaṭivu citaintu aḻintālum avaṟṟōṭotta aṟiviṉuruvaṅkaḷ iṟaivaṉ ṟiruvuḷḷat tiṉkaṇ aḻiyāvāy niṉṟu avaitammai mīṇṭummīṇṭun tōṟṟu viyā niṟkum, itu koṇṭu, māyaiyiṉ vaṭivu aḻital pōla, iṟaivaṉatu aṟivuruvu aḻivataṉṟeṉpa tūun teṟṟeṉa viḷaṅkum.
iṉṉum, aṟivuṭaiyōṉ eṉṟu uyarttuc collap paṭupavaṉ, tāṉ uṇarnta poruḷkaḷiṉ vaṭivaṅ kaḷō ṭotta uruvaṅkaḷait taṉ aṟiviṉkaṇ aiyantiri piṉṟi amaittuk koṇṭavaṉēyāva ṉeṉpatūum, aṟivillātōṉ eṉṟu iḻittuc collap paṭupavaṉ tāṉ kaṇṭa entap poruḷiṉ vaṭivattaiyun taṉṉuḷḷat tiṉkaṇ amaittuk koḷḷa māṭṭātavaṉēyāva ṉeṉpatūum, iṟaivaṉaṟivu immāyaiyiṟ kāṇappaṭum ellā vaṭivukaḷaiyum muṉṉarē yaṟinta uruvukaḷuṭaiyatā yiruppiṉallatu ipparupporuḷ vaṭivukaḷ tāmē tōṉṟāvākaliṉ iṟaivaṉatu pēraṟivu ivaiyellā vaṟṟaiyun taṉṉakat taṭakkiya uruvuṭaiya tāyē tikaḻu meṉpatūum piṟavuṅ kaṭavuḷukku aruḷuruvam uṇṭu eṉṉum ematu kaṭṭuraiyil naṉku viḷakkiyirukkiṉṟēm; avvirivai ataṉkaṭ kāṇmiṉkaḷ!
aḵtokkum; iṟaivaṉaṟivu uruvuṭaiyatāyē niṟkumeṉiṉ,ataṉai aruveṉṟu kūṟutal eṉṉai yeṉpārkku, uyirkaḷum uyirkaḷiṉ aṟivuṅ kaṇ mutaliya poṟikaḷukkup pulaṉākāmai paṟṟi aruveṉṟum, uyirkaḷiṉ aṟivu kaṭpulaṉākāviṭiṉum puṟapporuḷkaḷil tāṉkarutiya vaṭiviṉai amaittal  kaṇṭu ataṉaṟivu avvaṭiviṉōṭotta uruvuṭaiya teṉṟuṅ koḷḷutal pōla, iṟaivaṉum iṟaivaṉṟaṉpēr aṟivum poṟi kaḷukkup pulaṉākāmai paṟṟi aruveṉṟum,  aṅṅaṉa māyiṉum avaṉṟaṉ pēraṟivu eṇṇutaṟkaṭaṅkā ittaṉai yulakaṅkaḷaiyum ivvulakaṅkaḷiluḷḷa ittaṉai paṇṭaṅ kaḷaiyum ivaṟṟiliruntu ivai tammai nukartaṟku eṇṇiṟanta uyirkaḷōṭu kūṭiya ittaṉaiyuṭampukaḷaiyum amait tiruttal koṇṭu ivaṟṟiṉ vaṭivukaṭku mutalāṉa uruvukaḷ vāynta toṉṟākavēyirukkumeṉṟum viṭuttal vēṇṭum, eṉavē, aṟivellāmaḻiyā vuruvaṅ kaḷuṭaiya vāyēyirukku meṉpatūum, aṉṉavāyiṉum avai ūṉuṭampil amainta poṟikaḷiṉ vāyilāka aṟiyap paṭāmal mācaṟṟa aṟivuk kaṇ koṇṭē kāṇappaṭu meṉpatūum niṉaiviṟ patikkaṟ pālaṉavākum, tiruñāṉa campantar mūṉṟāṇṭaic ciṟumaka vāyirunta ñāṉṟu kaṇṭu pāṭiya iṟaivaṉuruvam,avar tantaiyār kaṇkaḷukkup pulaṉākāmal avarkkumaṭṭum pulaṉāṉa taṉmaiyaiyuṟṟu nōkkuṅkāl,avar kaṇṭa iṟaivaṉu ruvamaṟivuruvamē yallātu ūṉuruvam aṉṟeṉpatūum avarataṉaik kaṇṭatun tamatu aṟivukkaṇ koṇṭē yallātu ūṉakkaṇ koṇṭaṉ ṟeṉpatūum pakuttuṇarntu koḷḷal vēṇṭum, iṟaivaṉuruvam peṟṟiyatātal kaṇṭa aruṇanticivaṉār,
  māyai tāṉ malattaip paṟṟi varuvatōr vaṭiva mākum
  āyaā ṇavam akaṉṟa aṟivoṭu toḻilai yārkkum
  nāyakaṉ ellā ñāṉat toḻiṉmutal naṇṇa lālē
  kāyamō māyai yaṉṟu kāṇpatu catti taṉṉāl
eṉṟum,
cattitaṉ vaṭivēteṉṉil taṭaiyilā ñāṉamākum
eṉṟum aruḷic ceyvārāyiṉar. ākavē, iṟaivaṉatu pēraṟivu nilaiyai yuṇarvārākiya makkaḷiṉ ciṟṟaṟivuk kaṇṇukkē avaṉ aruvamākap pulaṉākātu niṟpaṉ; maṟṟu, avaṉ aruḷaic cārntu, ataṉāl mācu tīrntu tūyarāṉa aṉpariṉ tūya aṟivukku, avaṉṟaṉ tūya aṟivuruvanilai muṟṟum pulaṉāṉavāṟēyāy viḷaṅkā niṟkumeṉṟu uṇarntukoḷḷal vēṇṭum.
iṉi, meypporuḷāñ civam oṉṟē eṉṟatu, mēṟkāṭṭiyavāṟṟāṉ māṟun takaiyavāṉa poruḷkaḷum uyirkaḷum,māṟā nilaiyiṉatāṉa civattai nōkka meyyallātaṉavātal peṟappaṭutalāṟ, civam oṉṟē meyp poruḷātal teruṭṭiyavāṟu, aṟṟēṟ civamallāta poruḷkaḷellām poyyeṉa muṭintu ēkāṉmavāta mām piṟaveṉiṉ; aṅṅaṉamaṉṟu, nilai māṟātatē meyyeṉṟum, nilaimāṟuvatē poyyeṉṟum ulaka vaḻakkāṉuñ cāṉṟōr nūl vaḻakkāṉum naṉkaṟiyak kiṭattaliṉ, eṉṟumillāta veṟum pāḻaiyum poy yaiyum oṉṟeṉṟal aṭātu. nillātavaṟṟai nilaiyiṉa veṉṟuṇarum, pullaṟivu eṉṉun tirukkuṟaḷum ik karuttē paṟṟi yeḻuntatākum, allatūum, illāta veṟum pāḻaippaṟṟip pēcuvāruṅ kēṭpārum yāṇṭu milarāka lāṉum, uyirkaḷum poruḷkaḷumiruntē mupporu ḷārāycci nikaḻavēṇṭumākalāṉum,nilai māṟuvaṉa uḷporuḷkaḷē yallāmal il poruḷkaḷātal cellāmaiyāṉum, itaṉkaṇ ēkāṉma vātaṅ kūṟuvārurai oru ciṟitum poruntāteṉa viṭukka,
`civam eṉṉuñ col cemmai yeṉṉum paṇpaṭiyākap piṟantu civanta niṟattaiyum, iṉpattaiyum uṇarttuñ ciṟantatamiḻc collāmākalāṉum,iṟai vaṉṟaṉ aṟivuruc ciṟanta niṟattiṉatāyum iṉpa nilai yiṉatāyum irukkum uṇmaiyiṉai namakku niṉaivuṟuttik kāṇavuṅ karutavum paṭāta mutalvaṉṟaṉ aṟivuruviṉai nām kāṇavuṅ karutavum eḷiyatāmpaṭi vaittu namakku niṉaivorumai yiṉaip payappippatil iccoṟ pōlak kaṭavuḷukkuriya vēṟeccollum utavipurivatiṉmai yāṉuñ, civam eṉṉum iccollaiyē kaṭavuḷukkuc ciṟanta peyarāka vaittup paṇṭaik kālantoṭṭu iṉṟu kāṟum āṉṟōrkaḷ vaḻaṅki varutalāṉum, īṇṭu aṭikaḷārum accollaiyē ciṟantatāka eṭuttu aruḷic ceyvā rāyiṉar. aṭikaḷār piṟāṇṭuñ  civam piramamuṭiyē eṉṟu ōtuvatūuṅ karuttiṟ patikkaṟpāṟṟu,
iṉic, civam oṉṟē meypporuḷ eṉṟu āḻn tārāyntu kaṇṭa tollāciriyarkaḷum, avar vaḻi piḻai yātu vanta aṭikaḷārun tāṅkaṇṭa avvarum pēruṇmai yiṉai, makkaḷellāru muṇarntu uytal vēṇṭumeṉ ṟeḻunta pērirakkattāl aṭuttaṭutteṭut tuṇartti varavum, ataṉaik kaṭaippiṭiyātu, piṟantu perun tuṉpaṅkaḷiṟ paṭṭuḻaṉṟu iṟantu oḻinta aracar kaḷaiyum iḻinta makkaḷaiyumellān teyvaṅkaḷāka vaḻipaṭṭup piṟaviyaip pāḻpaṭutti iruḷ nirayattiṟkuñ celluṅ kīḻmakkaḷai atu ceytalākāteṉṟu mēlaic ceyyuḷiṉ piṉṉiraṇṭaṭikaḷil taṭuttaruḷiṉār.
iṉi, mēlaic ceyyuḷiṉ iṟutippakutiyil, iṟaivaṉ ellāvuyirkaḷiṉ akattē neñcattāmaraiyiṉ aṟivu veḷiyilum, puṟattē eṇṇiṟanta vulakaṅkaṭ kellāṅ kaḷaikaṇṇāmaruḷ veḷiyilum potuniṉṟu iṉpa āṭal puriyum iyalpiṉai, ñāṉāciriyaṉ meyyurai vaḻiniṉṟu mūccaiyaṭakki, mūlattiluḷḷa kuṇṭaliyai yeḻuppi, ataṉōṭu uṇarviṉai oruṅkiyaittuk koṇṭu, neñcat tāmaraiyiṉ akattē ceṉṟu kaṇṭu iṉpuṟum ñāṉayōkap payiṟciyuṭaiyārkku, ellā aṟivum ellāiṉpamum oruṅkeḻuntu, perukit tōṉṟum eṉṟaruḷic ceytu muṭittamai kāṇka! ivvuṇmai āciriyar tirumūlar aruḷic ceyta,
nācikku atōmukam paṉṉiraṇ ṭaṅkulam
  nīcittam vaittu niṉaiyavum vallaiyēl
  mācitti māyōkam vantu talaip peytun
  tēkattuk keṉṟuñ citaivillai yāmē
eṉṉun tiruppāṭṭāṉum naṉku teruṭṭappaṭṭa teṉka.
aṭikkuṟippu
1.	“Life does not appear without the operation of antecedent life” Fragments of Science by Prof. J. Tyndall, Vol.II. p. 299.
caivacittānta ñāṉapōtam
- muṟṟum -



Preface
“The Caiva Siddhantha system is the most elaborate, influential, and undoubtedly the most instrinsically valuable of all the religions of India. It is áeculiarly the South Indian, and Tamil religion. *** Caivism is the old prehistoric religion of South India, essentially existing from Pre - Aryan times, and holds sway over the hearts of the Tamil People”.
Dr. G.U. Pope in his English translation of the sacred Tamil lyrics, the Thiruvachakam of the great Saiva Saint Manicakavachakar, p. xxiv.
The above extract expresses the just estimate arrived at by that great Christian missionary and profound Tamil scholar Dr. G.U. Pope as regards the instrinsic merit and high antiquity of the Saiva Siddhanta the religion and philosophy of the Tamil people. Most of his active period of life Dr. Pope spent in the heart of the Tamil country moving intimately with all classes of the Tamil people and learning at first-hand not only their beliefs, customs and manners but also their ancient classics such as Thirukkural, Purananooru, Silappathikaram, Manimekalai, Palamoli, Naladiyar, Periapuranam, Sivajnanabodham and others. His erudition in Tamil was equal to, and in some respects even exceeded, the erudition of great native scholars. So his impartial estimate of the Saiva Siddhanta, coming as it does from a great western scholar who combined in himself the culture of both west and east, might, by being prefixed to this English Preface of the following work of mine on Saivism, serve well to impress its value and importance on the minds of those learned Indians and Europeans to whom it is a sealed book. For the modern philosophic and religious thought in the east and the west is so strongly coloured either by the atheistic system of the Buddhists or by the pantheistic system of Sankara and his cult, except where the most wholesome teachings of professor William James have pervaded, that almost all lines of inquiry either into the realm of matter or into the realm of spirit, might be said, without exaggeration, to end themselves either in a materialistic monism or in an idealistic monism that is to say, they affirm simply that in the end (the end of what is not clear from what they affirm) there exists nothing but what appears to be a form of energy whether you call it matter or spirit.
In whatever way this kind of monism so stubbornly help in the teeth of all human experience, may seem to satisfy certain “tough-minded” people, certain it is that it cannot satisfy the cravings of the “tender - minded” people, as the two types, namely the monists and the pluralist, have been so pertinently called by Prof. James. We who live in the world of both mind and matter, we who acquire all our knowledge and experince by our contact with so many intelligent beings higher and lower, with so many products of matter which minister unto our wants cannot rest content with such a nameless and formless and therefore such an incomprehensible monism, but need a system which would meet our wishes and aspirations in entire harmony with out past and present experiences.
While our worldly experience is thus manysided, it has yet a unity that underlies all its variety. In the words of James, “It is both one and many”, and therefore “let us adopt a sort of pluralistic monism”1 Now the Saiva Siddhanta furnishes us with such a pluralistic monism as is evident from the following quotation taken from one of the fourteen authoritative works in Tamil which treat of the Saiva Siddhanta system. According to this system.
“There are six entities which have no begninning. The first of these is the Lord (Pathi) Who is One. The second is the aggregate  of all Souls (Pasu) with their undeveloped potentialities of thought and act, interpenetrated by a divine but hidden influence. The third is the impurity of Anavam wearing the form of darkness. The fourth is twofold deeds (Vinai). The fifth and sixth are the two kinds of Mayai, the pure and impure the substratum and material of the phenomenal universe.”2
In the following work which comprises nine of my Tamil lectures delivered at different places and on different occasions. I have given to the best of my ability a clear and rational exposition of the views of the Saiva Siddhanta relating to the six entities enumerated above. For the guidance of English - knowing readers I will give here a succinct explanation of the six categories.
Saivism is strictly a monotheistic religion holding that only as God which is truth, intelligence, and bliss, which is above births and deaths, which is all love and grace towards all individual souls, which is distinct from both matter and finite selves and yet dwells within them and without and is ever active in bringing the souls out of darkness and setting them in its light and beatitude. It calls God by the name of Sivam, simply because this term specifies the essential quality of God which is pure love, pure bliss. In later times many more gods such as Subramanya, Ganesa, Veerabhadra and others were adopted into its fold but none of the founders of the Saiva religion held any one as God except Siva. In the ten collections of the sacred hymns or Thirumurai sung by St. Thirujnanasambandha, St, Appar, St. Sundarar, St. Manickavachakar and others, there is not a singly hymn sung in praise of any god other than Siva. And in each and every temple of Siva his only true emblem the Sivalinga is set up in central shrine as the pre-eminent object of first worship, while the images of all other gods are quartered around the central sanctuary and offered only subordinate places and ordinary worship. Thus you find that not only do the ancient sacred literature of Saivism but the very construction of its temples and the pre-eminent  form of worship daily offered in them to Siva also bear indisputable testimony to the monotheistic character of the Saiva religion.
According to Saivism God is as remote and transcendent as he is near and personal. Though his natural is one, it appears to be twofold to the finite conception of the individual minds, just as the sun which appears to our naked eyes like a small bright disc, is in reality, immense in its size and thus seems to present two aspects to our perception and conception. As has been so well pointed out bt Prof. James in the lecture on Philosophy in his epochmaking work: “The varieties of Religious Experience.” “that the transcendentalist reasonings fail to make religion universal,” this incomprehensible and inconceivable aspect of God can never appeal to a devout mind that is thirsting after to obtain atleast a glimpse of his presence and personality. In so far as God is will, intelligence and freedom, he cannot be an unfeeling, unintelligent and impersonal existence. Nowhere is the personal aspect of God which is in vital relation with all living beings is so strongly emphasized as is Saivism. Since he is in and near every vital being and is helping them all to develop their faculties of thought and feeling, life is held in it so sacred that taking it away from a man or a beast is looked upon by it as an unpardonable and sacrilegious crime. Hence the very word Saivism has come to be applied to a vegetarian mode of living also.
Now, it is this hankering of the human mind after the living personal relation of God to itself that has prompted it to feel deeply the need of setting up a physical image before its outer eyes as a means of setting up a corresponding mental image before its inner eyes. For all our knowledge of anything consists in bringing into our mind a mental image corresponding to that particular physical thing and storing it up permanently in our memory for after use. Blot that image out of your mind or forget it altoghether and you thereby lose the knowledge of it also once for all. In fact, a man of vast knowledge is distinguished from another man of small knowledge only by his power of forming in his mind as well as of fixing on his memory myriads of distinct mental images corresponding exactly to the forms of physical objects which he was observing attentively and nothing carefully. This psychological law pertaining to the formation and development of the human mind must disclose to us the necessity of keeping before our eyes an image made of clay or wood, stone or any mineral matter for the sake of raising up in our mind a clear image of God corresponding to it. For, is it not utterly impossible for us to conceive of an object that we have not previously seen or heard, touched or smelled or tasted? It is the deep-felt longing of the human mind for a concrete thing to meditate upon that has brought into existence a multitude of temples not only in the Tamil country where its number is comparatively very large but also in other ancient civilized countries such as Egypt, Babylonia, Chaldea in the west, and Mexico and Peru in the east. We cannot close this part of our subject relating to idol-worship better than in the words of Dr. Edward Caird taken from his masterly treatment of “The Evolution of Religion.” His words rus as follows:-
“To represent God as a mere object is, as we have seen, to express the divine in an inadequate form, in a form that, at least, cannot be made fully adequate to the idea; for the principle of unity in all objects and subjects cannot be properly represented as one object among others. But, at the same time, it is also true that in some sense the whole is involved in every part of the universe, and therefore any part of it may for a time be taken as a type of the whole. *** When the spiritual cannot yet be separated from the natural, it is of the highest importance that the natural object which represents the spiritual should be, as it were, transfigured by the imagination, so that it may, so far as possible, symbolically take the place of the spiritual.”3
Viewed in this  light how significant is the worship paid to Sivalinga in the great temples that burst so magnificently on the sight of the travellers all over the south of India, must become apparent to all who have a right - thinking mind. I cannot pursue the treatment of this topic farther in a short preface like this. For a detailed explanation of the origin and meaning of the Sivalinga- worship the reader is referred to my Tamil lectures on “The Image-worship” and “Sivalinga” and to my English lecture on “The Conception of God as Rudra”.
Now, as to the second entity of the Saiva Siddhanta: “The aggregate of all Souls.” From the tiniest and the most simple unicellular organism called protoplasm to the highest and the most complex organism called man there exist from all eternity countless individual souls each having a distinct character of its own and each undergoing different degrees of development in accordance with its nature capacity and effort. All these souls are not the principles created out of nothing according to some religions, nor are they the splintered pieces of God itself according to some other religions, nor do they constitute so many evolved centres of unintelligent matter according to some material sciences, nor could they be pure fictions imagined in the infinite absolute according to some rank idealism. On the other hand they are as eternal as God and not even a single soul could, by any known or unknown power or process, be reduced into nothing nor could any one of them be transmuted into any other of the group. According to the Saiva Siddhanta, and ‘the pluralistic, as according to the Leibnizian view “says Dr. James Ward in his remarkable Gifford Lectures, “all the individuals there are have existed from the first and will continue to exist indefinitely.”4 And the same authority, like the Siddhanta, holds tenaciously to the view that “individuals who have no ‘doubles’, whose like all in all we never shall meet again”,5 In upholding the eternal reality of inidividual souls Dr. F.C.S. Schiller has said even more emphatically as follows:
“The ultimate self-existence of spirits, the doctrine that existence are many, spirits uncreated, uncaused, that are and ever have been and can never cease to be, is the only metaphysical ground for asserting the immortality of the individual. And this metaphysical ground we have secured by the preference given to Pluralism over Monism.”6
Now, as regards the third principle the impure and dark Anavam, what the Saiva Siddhanta says is in the words of Dr. F.C.S. Schiller this: “Evil was potentially existent in the world” and “the world was created in order to remedy this pre-existent and precosmic defect.”7 If all had been perfect eternally like God, creation is unnecessary, and it may even be mischievous, like the mischievous actions of some wild boys who take plesure in throwing stones on innocent animals and even on grown-up persons. But such does not seem to be the case since all souls have defects in various grades and degrees which with the help of creation they strive and struggle to remove. This supreme purpose of creation cannot be in the least understood, unless, you recognize the potential existence of the evil principle called Anavam in the Saiva Siddhanta. Of this evil principle Prof. Henry Drummond the great scientist and theologian writes:
“There is a natural principle in man lowering him, deadening him, pulling him down by inches to the mere animal plane, blinding reason, searing conscience, paralysing will.”8
So far as my knowledge of various religions and various systems of philosophy goes, I venture to say that I have not come across any other religion or philosophy in which this root-cause of creation, the pre-existence of the evil principle is recognized and explicitly mentioned as in the system of Saiva Siddhanta. Of course, there is a hint of it in the first chapter of the Book of Genesis in the Old Testament as has been so penetratingly pointed out by Dr. F.C.S. Schiller, but it has unfortunately escaped the notice of almost all commentators on the book.
Now, let us turn to the fourth category: the twofold deeds good and bad. Every life from amoeba upwards is endowed with intelligence and this enables all organisms from the lowest to the highest to respond to the impressions proceeding from surrounding objects and perform certain functions either in opposition to, or in concurrence with, those impressions. In answering thus to outside calls the lower organisms learn to repeat what is favourable at first to the maintenance of their own life and afterwards to the maintenance of the life of their own offspring. The first act of every living being meets with great resistance offered by both the inside and outside things, that is, by its own body and by the things external to it. All the products of matter do not yield readily to the will of an intelligent being, unless it exercises its intelligence constantly and devises newer and newer means to bend them to its will. In this way it learns that not one act but many acts of which the every succeding one is more intelligently performed than the preceding one, are necessary for achieving the conquest over matter.
It is such repeatged actions of living beings that are collectively called Karmas or Vinai in Saivism; or in the language of psychology habits. As we observe the infinite grades of lives, and the infinite degrees of intelligence, we are led to believe that no man who gains an insight into the nature and evolution of individual souls can at a bound conclude that the life of a soul can become perfect in a single birth when even the duration of which varies infinitely from a moment to an age.
Further, the actions of living beings acquire moral value only after they ascend to human existence in the scale of life. Therefore it seems most unreasonable to assert that the lower lives will become extinct after they have passed through a single existence and before they have reached the higher stages at which their actions acquire moral worth. Even after reaching the human stage sould do differ so widely among themselves in the amount of intelligence they exhibit that, here too, perfection is not attainable by all in a single birth. And so before attaining to the stage of perfection, it is reasonable to hold with the Saiva Siddhanta that all souls must accumulate habits after habits only by passing through myriads of bodily existences in this world and in others that are distant from ours. As has been put by Dr. James Ward, “At any rate `metempsychosis’ in some form seems an unavoidable corollary of thorough - going pampsychism, so long as we look broadly at the facts of life as a whole.”9
Come we now finally to the fifth and the sixth categories, the pure and the impure Mayai. Modern science has discovered that the world of our experience is ultimately resolvable into protons and electrons. The impure Mayai is the direct material cause of the universe, while the pure: the protons and electrons, constitutes only indirectly so. The reason for this two fold classification of Mayai is this. The souls buried, as it were, in coarse and foul impurities require equally coarse substances to be cleansed of their impurities: whereas the highly developed souls rising above the filth require only the help of finer substances to raise them still higher. For we see plainly before our eyes the mental powers of individual souls developing in different degrees in different souls without any limit being set to them. In proportion to the mental development of souls, matter which serves as its vehicle also becomes rarified in different degrees. In the words of Dr. Schiller, “The spiritualization of Matter is displayed also in its relations to spiritual beings. As in the course of evolution these become more harmonized with the Divine Will, Matter the expression of that Will, becomes more and more harmonized with the desires of spiritual beings.”10 I need then hardly say that the doctrine of Mayai as held by Saivism is in entire harmony with the discoveries of modern science.
To all those who seek after an exact knowledge of these six eternal verities, it must be very gratifying to note as above that the recent developments of western philosophy and physical science go every inch to prove the truth and value of all the fundamental doctrines only of the Saiva Siddhanta and not of any other religion or philosophy.

mūṉṟām patippiṉ mukavurai
kaṟṟavarmutaṟ kallātavar īṟāṉa ettiṟattavarkkum iṉitu viḷaṅkumporuṭṭup paṟpala ūrkaḷilum paṟpala nakarkaḷilum avaikkaḷaṅkaḷil yāmnikaḻttiya caiva cittāntac coṟpoḻivukaḷiṉ oru tokutiyē `caiva cittānta ñāṉapōta mutaṟpākam eṉṉum in nūliṉkaṭ perumpālum aṭaṅki yirukkiṉṟaṉa.
innūliṉ mutaṟpatippākak karutappaṭum emmuṭaiya coṟpoḻivukaḷ cila 1906, 1915ām āṇṭukaḷil veḷivanta ematu ñāṉacākarattiṉ itaḻkaḷil veḷip pōntaṉavākum, avai appōtu muṉpaṉikkāla upanniyācaṅkaḷ eṉṉum peyarāl taṉinūlākavum veḷivantaṉa. annūlil aṭaṅki yavai āṟu virivāṉa coṟpoḻivu kaḷēyākum,
appatippiṉ paṭikaḷ celavāṉapiṉ, 1922ām āṇṭil piṉṉum pattuc coṟpoḻivukaḷ cērkkappeṟṟu, āka mottam patiṉāru coṟpoḻivukaḷ aṭaṅkiya itaṉ iraṇṭām patippu veḷi varalāyiṟṟu,
avviraṇṭām patippup paṭikaḷ celavaḻintapiṉ, itaṉ mūṉṟām patippai acciṭat tuvaṅkip pala āṇṭukaḷ kaḻin tamaiyāṉum, ivaṟṟiṭaiyē iṉṉum pala coṟpoḻivu kaḷaic cērkka vēṇṭuvatu iṉṟiyamaiyātatāy nērntamai yāṉum, ic coṟpoḻivukaḷai yellāmoruṅkē cērppiṉ innūl aṟunūṟu pakkaṅkaḷukku mēṟ perukumeṉak kaṇṭamai yāṉum oṉpatu coṟpoḻivukaḷ tokutta innūliṉ mutaṟ pākattai ippōtu veḷiyiṭalāṉēm, ēṉaic coṟpoḻi vukaḷ tokutta iraṇṭāmpākam iṉip patippiṭṭu veḷiyiṭappaṭum,
emmuṭaiya nūlkaḷiṟ ciṟupāṉmaiyākak kāṇappaṭum vaṭacoṟkaḷaiyum muṟṟakkaḷaintu, tūya tamiḻc coṟkaḷaiyē avvavviṭaṅkaḷil nirappi ippōtu patippiṭṭu varum ematu muṟaippaṭi, innūliṉ muṟpatippil veḷivanta virivurai kaḷilirunta vaṭacoṟkaḷaik kaḷaintu āṅkāṅkut tūyatamiḻc coṟkaḷaip peytum, ippōtu itaṉkaṭ putiyavākac cērkka eṭutta virivuraikaḷait tūya taṉittamiḻilēyē eḻutiyum innūlai iyaṟṟalāṉēm.
āṉālum, ittamiḻ nāṭṭiṟ putiyarāyp pukunta āriyar, pauttar, camaṇar, māyāvāta vētāntikaḷ, vaiṇavar eṉpārellān, tamiḻ makkaḷai maruṭṭutaṟ poruṭṭu,avar vaḻaṅkun tamiḻmoḻiyiṟ ṟammuṭaiya kōṭpāṭukaḷai varaintu vaiyāmal, avar aṟiyāta vaṭamoḻiyil avai tammai varaintu vaittu,avaikaḷaimaṟaiporuḷākavē parappi vantamaiyāl,aṉṉār ceyta ēmāṟṟattiṉiṉṟun tamiḻ makkaḷai viṭuvikkak karutiya piṟkālattut tamiḻp periyār kaḷum, paṇṭaittamiḻc cāṉṟōr kaṇṭa muṭinta vuṇmai kaḷukkuc caiva cittāntam eṉa vaṭamoḻiyiṟ ciṟappup peyar niṟuviyum, avvuṇmaikaḷai viḷakkuṅkāṟ paṟpala kuṟiyīṭukaḷai vaṭacoṟ koṇṭē putiyavāyc camaittum, vaṭamoḻi yilēyē `civākamaṅkaḷ, `civapurāṇaṅkaḷ iyaṟṟiyun, tamiḻc cāṉṟōr tamatu mey yaṟivāṟ ṟeḷinta meyp poruḷkaḷai it tamiḻ nāṭṭakattē paravac ceyyalāyiṉar. ataṉāl, aṅṅaṉañ ciṟappup peyarkaḷākavuṅ kuṟiyīṭukaḷākavum pukuntu tamiḻ nūlkaḷil nilaipeṟṟa vaṭacoṟkaḷ coṟṟoṭar kaḷaic caṭutiyil māṟṟiṉāl avaṟṟiṟ paḻakiṉārkku innūṟ poruḷ mayakkattait tarumeṉak karuti, avaiyiṟṟai maṭṭum māṟṟātu āṅkāṅku irukkaviṭṭēm. āṉālum, avai tamakku nērāṉa taṉittamiḻc coṟkaḷaiyum innūliṉ iṟutiyiṟ kāṭṭiyirukkiṉṟēm. aṅṅaṉaṅ kāṭṭap paṭṭirukkun tamiḻccoṟkaḷ naṉku vaḻaṅki varalāyiṉapiṉ, avvaṭamoḻic ciṟappup peyarkaḷ coṟkaḷ coṟ ṟoṭarkaḷaiyuṅ kaḷaintuviṭak karutiyirukkiṉṟēm, itukāṟum utavi ceyta civapirāṉ iṉṉum aṭiyēmukku utavuka!
